# World #8 (Latin Pastoral-Congregational Christianity) — Decision Log

**World-code:** `lpc` (Latin Pastoral-Congregational Christianity), assigned at Step 0, this world's first document.

This log holds project-lead decisions, escalation resolutions, and revision rationale for this world's build, so the construction documents themselves stay clean, substantive content. Review artifacts (the full adversarial review text) live in `Review-Artifacts/`; this log records project-lead-level decisions specifically, per CO-022's own rule that nothing may be attributed to the project lead anywhere in this build without a verifiable record — held to the same bar as Frozen.

---

## Entries

### 2026-09-01 — Doc_01, "orthogonality to state power" framing: project-lead instruction on method

**Context.** Doc_01's own Round 5 independent adversarial review found that the document's World #6 (Imperial and Juridical Christianity) boundary discharge had come to depend on choosing among three competing interpretive readings of a clause in the portfolio-level Step 0 Conclusion's World #8 entry — "confirmed distinct from world #6 (non-overlapping authority structure, **orthogonality to state power**, temporal overlap rather than sequence)." Round 5 held that choosing among the three readings was a decision about a portfolio-level determination that this build thread could not make unilaterally, and recommended escalating the question to the project lead: which reading governs, given that Augustine (one of this world's two anchor figures) is independently documented soliciting Roman state coercion against the Donatists, and that the neighboring, already-built and cleared World #6 (Imperial and Juridical Christianity)'s own completed Doc_04 found the church-state relationship a co-equal, cross-strand Primary gravity for that world.

**The question put to the project lead, in substance:** which of three readings of "orthogonality to state power" should govern this world's own boundary section — (1) a narrow claim that the office is literally never touched by state power (already known to be false and not defended); (2) a narrower claim about the *ground* of episcopal office, i.e. that the office's own legitimacy is not constituted by a relationship to state power, even where the office instrumentally solicits it; or (3) a broader "organizing axis" reading, under which this world's formation logic would need to be shown not to be centered on its relationship to state power at all.

**The project lead's response (Mark, 2026-09-01, in this build session's own conversation, quoted verbatim):**

> "our principle is always truth, transparency in good and grounded in the sources (we dont fabricate or midigate) i try very hard not to influence the sources"

And, when asked to confirm this reframing before treating it as settled:

> "is this a part of the world source and ecology, we are truthful about how the sources present the christian tradistion, but we are not tied to a single influencer/scholor from the time, it is a living ecology that doesn't alway have consistancy. we just say that, augustine did this in the begining then changed his approach and did that"

**What this instruction actually settles, stated narrowly rather than inferred broadly.** The project lead did not select one of the three offered readings. The instruction is about *method*: this project's own governing practice, for this document and generally, is to report what the primary sources document — including where a figure's or a world's own relationship to a question changed over time, or was never consistent — rather than to resolve that record into a single theory manufactured to fit a portfolio-level screening phrase. This is a standing instruction about how sourced claims are made in this build, consistent with Constitution Article 15's own principle that formation worlds are "historically developing ecclesial realities shaped, to the degree the evidence attests, through instability, adaptation, and incomplete continuity." It is not, on its own terms, a ruling that no escalation category applies to Doc_01's disposition — that is a separate question under CO-022, to be assessed on the document's own merits against the actual escalation-category text, not inferred from this instruction alone.

**Consequence for Doc_01, in two steps rather than one, matching Doc_01 §9's own segregation of them.** First, immediately on this instruction (the "Post-Round-5, pre-Round-6" revision): §7's World #6 boundary discharge was rewritten to report Cyprian's and Augustine's own differing relationships to state power rather than adjudicate which of three prior readings of "orthogonality to state power" is correct, and §9's escalation-category assessment was revised on the premise that this reframing removes the triggers the three-readings framing had created. Second, at Round 6, independent adversarial review (`Review-Artifacts/Doc01_Round6_Review.md`) found that first revision's own account of the arc itself factually wrong — compressed into "present, but late," when Letter 185 §25 shows Augustine already argued for a real, narrower solicitation of state power — though, in the event, not granted (Letter 185 §26) — early in his own episcopate (a council NPNF's own editorial note dates 401), before the later, broader position Letter XCIII §17 records him arguing for after a change of mind (a.d. 408) — and §7 was revised again on that finding. This entry's own wording of the arc is kept in step with Doc_01 §7's own current formulation, per CO-022's *Cross-document fact consistency* rule; both steps are checked independently, not assumed, per this project's own review discipline. See `Review-Artifacts/Doc01_Round6_Review.md`, `Doc01_Round7_Review.md` and `Doc01_Round8_Review.md` for the independent adversarial checks this reasoning received and the further revisions each required.

**Disposition status of this specific item:** see Doc_01 §9 for the current, independently-reviewed escalation-category assessment. This entry records the project-lead instruction that occasioned the revision; it does not itself constitute Doc_01's disposition.

### 2026-09-01 — Cross-document correction: Cyprian's "your suffrage and God's judgment" is Epistle XXXIX, not Epistle XL

**Context.** Doc_01's own Round 9 revision (§5, the Ecological-orientation bullet) cited Cyprian's own letter to his people on the Felicissimus schism — "your suffrage and God's judgment," against a rival faction's "ancient venom" toward his episcopate — as "Ep. XL." Doc_01 §9's own revision log (the Round 9 entry) repeated the same citation twice in describing the fix. Round 9's independent adversarial review (`Doc01_Round9_Review.md`) certified the surrounding passage without re-verifying this specific locus against the vendored text, and Doc_01 reached Approved to proceed with the misattribution live in both its narrative text and its own revision history.

**How it was found.** During Doc_02's own Step 2 work (Source Ecology / Source Registry), the citation was carried into `Source_Registry.md` row 1 with a fresh claim that it had been "directly verified against `anf05` in Doc_01's own Round 9 review." Doc_02's own Round 1 independent adversarial review re-checked the claim at source rather than accepting the inherited certification, and found both phrases occur inside `div3 id="iv.iv.xxxix"` (Epistle XXXIX, "To the People, Concerning Five Schismatic Presbyters of the Faction of Felicissimus") — not Epistle XL, "To Cornelius, on His Refusal to Receive Novatian's Ordination," a different letter on an unrelated subject that contains neither phrase. Independently re-verified against `cic/texts/anf05_hippolytus-cyprian-caius-novatian.xml` before this entry was written, per this project's own standing discipline of never accepting a review finding without re-deriving it.

**Consequence.** Doc_01 §5 (live text) and §9 (the Round 9 log, two instances) were corrected in place, with a dated inline note at each site pointing to this entry, per CO-022's *Naming and term propagation* rule. `Doc01_Round9_Review.md` itself is left unchanged, as the historical record of what that round actually certified — the error, not this correction, is what that file documents having happened. `Doc_02_Source_Ecology.md` and `Source_Registry.md` are corrected in the same pass this entry records. The underlying substantive finding — that Cyprian's own words ground his office in "suffrage and God's judgment" rather than the state's favor — is unaffected; only the citation was wrong.

**Escalation check.** This is a citation-accuracy correction to an already-Approved-to-proceed document, not a reopening of any finding, conclusion, or disposition Doc_01 reached — the strand-singular finding, the World #6 boundary discharge, and the escalation-category assessment all stand as before. No CO-022 escalation category applies to a correction of this kind (it changes no claim's substance, only a citation), consistent with CO-022's own *Coach verification* exception for "small, purely technical corrections... provided the correction changes no substantive claim, finding, or decision." Logged here per the same exception's own disclosure requirement, even though this correction was made by the build thread itself (still inside its own world-build folder) rather than by a coach thread.

### 2026-09-01 — Round 1/Round 2 disagreement: `donatism.yaml` does contain an Optatus entry; an unre-derived review finding reached a live document

**Context.** Doc_02's own Round 1 independent adversarial review (`Review-Artifacts/Doc02_Round1_Review.md`, finding H2) found, correctly, that an earlier draft of Doc_02 §1 had the Optatus placement backwards (claiming he was homed on the sibling Donatism build rather than this one). In the course of that finding, Round 1 also asserted, as a supporting claim, that the sibling build's own corpus map (`donatism.yaml`) "does not contain him at all." The Round 1 fix pass adopted that supporting claim into Doc_02 §1's own revised text without independently re-deriving it — a lapse in this project's own standing discipline of never accepting a review finding without re-checking it at source.

**How it was found.** Doc_02's own Round 2 independent adversarial review (`Review-Artifacts/Doc02_Round2_Review.md`, finding H1) independently re-parsed `donatism.yaml` and found the supporting claim false: the file carries its own Optatus entry (`author: optatus`, `role: context`, `confidence: assigned`, note "Assigned twice with different roles, deliberately"), unchanged since commit `4c4a6541` (2026-08-27) — well before Round 1 ran — and the sibling Donatism build's own Doc_02 §1 and Registry row 1 cite that very entry directly. Independently re-verified a third time by this build thread before writing this entry and the corresponding fixes: `git log` on the file, a direct parse of its own YAML, and a direct read of the sibling branch's own Doc_02 and Registry all confirm the entry's existence and content.

**Consequence.** The census has therefore already answered the question Doc_01 §8 item 2 posed to this step (re-home, hold, or double-place Optatus): it is already double-placed, deliberately, on its own recorded reasoning — `tradition` on this world's own map, `context` on the sibling Donatism build's. `Doc_02_Source_Ecology.md` §1 and §10, and `Source_Registry.md` row 27, are corrected in the same pass this entry records, each with an inline note. The correction changes a fact about what a companion world's generated corpus-map file contains; it does not change this document's own position that Optatus is not drawn on for any specific claim in this world's own construction, and nothing in Doc_01's or Doc_02's own substantive findings depends on which way the correction runs.

**A second, unrelated correction from the same review round, recorded here rather than in a separate entry since both were caught together.** Round 2 also found that `Source_Acquisition_Manifest.md`'s G3 candidate (Possidius's *Vita Augustini*) named the wrong Internet Archive identifier: `archive.org/details/PossidiusAug` is a 2008 audio recording of Weiskotten's translation, not the scanned bilingual text edition, which is a separate item, `archive.org/details/sanctiaugustiniv00possrich`. Independently re-confirmed by live web search before this entry was written. The work, editor, press, year, and public-domain status G3 states were all already correct; only the identifier was wrong. Corrected at the Manifest and at Registry row 45.

**Escalation check.** Neither correction changes a claim's substance, a sourcing conclusion this world's own construction rests on, or any disposition — Doc_02, the Registry, and the Manifest remain undisposed drafts at the time of this entry, mid-review-cycle, so there is no cleared document being reopened. CO-022's fourth escalation category (unresolved tensions) has a specific limb for two reviews disagreeing with each other; this is exactly that case, and it is closed, not left open, by returning to the primary artifact (the corpus-map file itself, its `git log`, and the sibling branch's own record) rather than by preferring the more recent review on authority alone — consistent with Doc_01 §9's own established handling of the same limb. No escalation category applies. Logged here because CO-022's own rule requires a review disagreement to be recorded rather than silently resolved in the prose it corrects, and because an unre-derived claim reaching a live document has already happened once before in this world's build (the Ep. XL/Epistle XXXIX citation, logged above) — a pattern worth naming plainly for whoever next relies on a review finding in this build without re-deriving it themselves. (This entry's own count of "the second time" is superseded: this log no longer states a running total of the pattern's own instances at all, for the reason the "Pattern instances found by Round 4" entry below gives when it makes that change — each instance is identified by what it is and which entry records it, not by an ordinal, and no pointer here should be read as naming a current count.)

### 2026-09-01 — Second post-disposition Doc_01 edit (stale review-artifact count); and a build-thread-versus-review disagreement, running in the other direction

**Context — two items caught together by Doc_02's own Round 3 independent adversarial review, recorded together here since both are small, both are disclosure completeness rather than substance, and both extend a record already opened above.**

**First: Doc_01 was edited a second time after its own 2026-09-01 self-disposition, and neither the status line's own post-disposition note nor this log recorded it fully.** The first post-disposition edit (the Ep. XL/Epistle XXXIX citation fix, logged above) was disclosed correctly at the time. A second, separate post-disposition edit followed during Doc_02's own Round 2 fix pass: Doc_01 §9's own review-artifacts paragraph had gone stale after Round 9 — it still read "all eight rounds," naming only Rounds 1–8, though Round 9's own file (`Doc01_Round9_Review.md`) already existed and had already been cited elsewhere in the same section. This was corrected (now "all nine rounds," all nine filenames), and the status line was given a post-disposition note describing it — but that note described only the first edit (the citation fix), not this second one, leaving a reader who follows the status line's own pointer to this log without finding the second edit recorded anywhere except inside the paragraph it changed. Both edits are small, technical, and change no substantive claim, finding, or decision Doc_01 reached — consistent with the *Coach verification* exception both times — but the disclosure obligation applies to the fact of the edit, not only to edits of one particular kind, and this entry closes that gap.

**Second: Round 2's own M4 finding was itself wrong, and the build thread was right to reject it — a disagreement CO-022's own rule requires logging, not just correcting in the prose it touched.** During the Round 2 fix pass, Round 2's M4 asserted that the Donatist-patrimony claim (that the Donatists themselves appealed to the 256 council's own baptismal doctrine as their own patrimony) was "available and cheap" to re-ground because "the sibling build's own Doc_02 §1 and Registry document it from Optatus and Augustine directly." The build thread checked this before writing it into a live document — per this project's own standing discipline — and found it false: the string "patrimony" appears nowhere in the sibling Donatism build's own Doc_01, Doc_02, Registry, or Manifest; the only related statement in that build's own record is inside its Step 0, itself quoting `donatism.yaml`'s own corpus-map note, not an independent primary-source claim. The build thread correctly declined Round 2's suggested fix on this basis. Doc_02's own Round 3 independent adversarial review re-derived the same conclusion independently (fetching the sibling branch and grepping its record a third time) and confirmed the build thread's rejection was correct — but also found that the build thread's own *substitute* conclusion, reached in rejecting Round 2's fix, was itself wrong in a new way: it asserted, as a checked result, that the underlying claim "is not independently documented" anywhere, when in fact it is documented at length in this world's own vendored `npnf104` (Augustine's *On Baptism, Against the Donatists*, Registry row 13, Confidence A) — a search that had been run against the sibling build's own folder and never against `cic/texts/`. See Round 3's own H1 for the full finding; `Doc_02_Source_Ecology.md` §2 and `Source_Registry.md` rows 13 and 42 are corrected in the same pass this entry records.

**Escalation check.** Neither item changes a disposition (Doc_01 remains Approved to proceed on unaffected grounds; Doc_02/Registry/Manifest remain undisposed drafts). No CO-022 category applies to either. Logged per CO-022's own *Revision decision* rule that a review disagreement is recorded rather than resolved silently inside the prose it corrects. **This closing sentence is corrected here (Round 4's own L4): an earlier draft of it misdescribed its own second instance, in a way this entry's own body, two paragraphs above, gets right.** Stated precisely: this is the **second** instance of an unre-derived claim reaching a live document in this world's build — the first is Round 1's own `donatism.yaml` claim (logged in the entry above), which the Round 1 fix pass adopted into Doc_02 without re-deriving it, and Round 2 caught. Round 2's own sibling-build claim did **not** itself reach a live document — the build thread checked it, per this project's own standing discipline, found it false, and declined it, as this entry's own body already states. What reached the live document instead was the build thread's **own substitute negative**, formed in the course of correctly rejecting Round 2's claim, without searching `cic/texts/` — that is the second instance, and it is the build thread's own error, not a review's. (Two further instances, discovered after this entry was first written, are logged in the next entry below rather than folded in here.) Naming the pattern precisely, rather than approximately, is the same discipline the pattern itself is about: independent re-verification at source has caught a real error at every single round of this document set's review so far, including rounds whose own stated purpose was to catch exactly this kind of thing, and including, now, this entry's own closing summary of itself — which is the discipline working as designed, not a sign it can be relaxed.

### 2026-09-02 — Pattern instances found by Round 4: Round 3's own H1 locus description, and Entry 4's own closing-sentence miscount

**Context.** Doc_02's own Round 4 independent adversarial review found two instances of this world's build's own recurring pattern (an unre-derived claim reaching a live document), beyond the ones Entry 4 above records.

**Instance.** Round 3's own review artifact, in the course of correctly re-grounding the Donatist-patrimony claim on Augustine's *On Baptism, Against the Donatists* (Registry row 13), described one of its five supporting loci as "the *Retractations* preface (`v.iv.ii`, line 10796)." The Round 3 fix pass adopted that description verbatim into Registry row 13 and `Doc_02_Source_Ecology.md` §2, without independently re-deriving what `div3 id="v.iv.ii"` actually is. Round 4 found: it is titled "Preface." — NPNF's own preface to *On Baptism*, not to the *Retractations*, which is not vendored in this corpus at all — and the words quoted there are Augustine's, from *Retractations* II.18, reaching this volume only as the NPNF editor's own quotation. Two further of the same row's stated loci (Books II, VI, VII, cited as the work's own "titles") are likewise the vendored edition's own editorial *argumenta*, not Augustine's words. The underlying claim survives intact — Book III ch. 2 §2 is Augustine's own continuous text and carries it — but the row's own account of what had been "directly re-verified" was wrong about three of its four supporting elements. Corrected at Registry row 13 and Doc_02 §2 in the same pass this entry records.

**A second instance, inside this log itself.** Entry 4 above, in the course of correctly describing its own second item, itself misdescribed it: its closing sentence said the pattern had occurred three times, naming "Round 2's own sibling-build claim" as the second instance's own subject — but Round 2's claim did not reach a live document, and Entry 4's own body, two paragraphs earlier, already said so correctly. What reached the live document was the build thread's own substitute negative, not Round 2's claim. Round 4 caught this contradiction inside the Decision Log itself. Corrected in place, at Entry 4 above.

**Consequence.** No disposition is reopened — Doc_02, the Registry, and the Manifest remain undisposed drafts, and no finding, conclusion or disposition of Doc_01's is touched by anything in this entry.

**Escalation check.** No CO-022 category applies. Logged per CO-022's own *Revision decision* rule. (A note on this log's own practice, added retrospectively: earlier entries tried to keep a single running numeric count of how many times this pattern had recurred. That attempt was itself wrong more than once — see the entry after this one — so this log no longer states a total. Each instance is recorded on its own terms, identified by what it is and which entry records it, not by an ordinal.)

### 2026-09-02 — Doc_01 pointer correction (Round 5 fix pass); sibling-build cross-world state corrected; Round 4's own H1 and L1 miscounts

**Heading corrected here (Round 6's own M4): an earlier version of this heading called this Doc_01 edit "the third post-disposition" one, which the entry below dated 2026-09-02 and titled "Two more Decision Log pointer/count defects found by Round 6..." found was, in the same commit, also said of a different edit — retitled by what this edit actually did rather than by an ordinal that turned out not to be stable.**

**Context.** Doc_02's own Round 5 independent adversarial review found: (1) Doc_01's status line and §9 post-disposition note, corrected during the Round 4 fix pass (immediately above) to describe two post-disposition edits, pointed at the wrong Decision Log entries — corrected in place at Doc_01; (2) Round 4's own review artifact stated its H1 finding's count two ways ("two more... loci" in its heading, "three of its four... limbs" in its body) and Registry row 13's fix pass took the lower, wrong number, propagated to both Status lines; separately, Round 4's own L1 enumerated ten Registry rows and called them "the nine-row precedent," and the fix pass built its answering paragraph around "All nine," which resolves under no grouping; (3) this world's own Manifest and Registry made present-tense claims about the sibling Donatism build's own outstanding requests (Ziwsa's Optatus edition, Monceaux Tome V, the *CIL* VIII Numidia supplement) that had in fact been discharged on that branch the day before this document set's Round 4 revision was written.

None reopens a disposition or a substantive finding — each is a count, a pointer, or a currency correction.

**Escalation check.** No disposition is reopened — Doc_02, the Registry, and the Manifest remain undisposed drafts. This entry's own commit also edited Doc_01 (correcting its post-disposition note's count and Decision Log pointers) — recorded here rather than asserting Doc_01 was unaffected, which an earlier draft of this sentence wrongly did; that edit is one of the four Doc_01's own status line now counts by what it changed rather than by an ordinal (see the entry below for why the ordinal was dropped). No CO-022 category applies. Logged per CO-022's own *Revision decision* rule; this is a further instance of the entry above's own named pattern, and no attempt is made here to state a running total of it, for the reason given there.

### 2026-09-02 — Two more Decision Log pointer/count defects found by Round 6, and a structural fix to how this log cross-references itself

**Context.** Doc_02's own Round 6 independent adversarial review found that the two entries immediately above this one were inserted out of chronological order (the "Third post-disposition Doc_01 edit" entry, recording the Round 5 fix pass, had been placed *before* the "Pattern instances found by Round 4" entry, which records the earlier Round 4 fix pass) and that both entries' own pointers and counts, written against that wrong order, were themselves wrong: Doc_01's pointer to "the Decision Log's sixth entry" resolved to the wrong entry; the log stated both `8360e103` and `ce8246f2` as "the third post-disposition edit" in two places in the same commit; and `git log --follow` on Doc_01 shows **four** post-disposition edits (the citation fix; the stale round-count fix; the Round 4 fix-pass edit; the Round 5 fix-pass edit), not three.

**Consequence.** The "Second post-disposition Doc_01 edit" entry (dated 2026-09-01) and the "Pattern instances found by Round 4" entry (dated 2026-09-02) are now in chronological order. Doc_01's own status line and §9 post-disposition note are corrected to state **four** post-disposition edits and to identify each by what it changed rather than by a Decision Log entry number, since a numbered pointer into this log has now gone stale twice from insertions alone. **From this entry ("Two more Decision Log pointer/count defects found by Round 6...") forward, this log's own directional language ("above," "below," "the next entry," "the most recent entry," "the entry after this one") is retired in favor of dated, titled pointers, for the same reason — checked and enforced at each later entry, not merely declared here (Round 7's own M2 found this declaration itself violated twice, in the very entries added alongside it; both are corrected in place).** The six entries before this one are left as the historical record of what they said at the time rather than retroactively rewritten; a reader relying on this log's own cross-references should treat "above"/"below" language in any of those six as informal, and every entry from this one onward as using dated/titled pointers only.

**Escalation check.** No disposition is reopened. No CO-022 category applies. This is a further instance of the pattern the two entries above record, this time originating in the log's own insertion order rather than in a review artifact or a build-thread claim — logged per the same *Revision decision* rule, with no running total stated, consistent with this log's own current practice.

### 2026-09-02 — Project-lead decision: the *Codex Theodosianus* file supplied this session is referenced, not vendored

**Context.** The project lead supplied, directly in this build session, a TEI-encoded file: Oxford Text Archive item 0329, the full 16-book Latin text of the *Codex Theodosianus* (Mommsen and Meyer's own critical edition, *Theodosiani libri XVI cum Constitutionibus Sirmondianis*, 2nd ed., Berlin: Weidmann, 1954 photostat reprint of the 1905 original). This is the edition the sibling Donatism build's own G3 request named and, per Registry row 44's own corrected account (Round 6's own M8), failed to acquire after five attempts.

**The question put to the project lead, and the answer.** OTA distributes this specific file under a Creative Commons Attribution-NonCommercial-ShareAlike 3.0 licence — not a public-domain declaration, and `cic/texts/README.md`'s own vendoring rule requires out-of-copyright status for anything committed to `cic/texts/`, with in-copyright material "referenced by `source` record and never vendored." Asked how to handle the tension, the project lead chose: **"Reference only, don't commit the file"** — treat it under the vendoring rule's own stated alternative for in-copyright material, citable but never committed to `cic/texts/`.

**Consequence.** Registry row 44 is corrected to name this specific edition and identifier (`http://purl.ox.ac.uk/ota/0329`), state its licence plainly, and record it as Native, Confidence B, not a vendoring candidate. `Doc_02_Source_Ecology.md` §5 is corrected to match. The file itself is not added to `cic/texts/`. This document set does not currently draw on the *Codex Theodosianus* for any specific claim (row 44's own Licensed-For), so the correction changes no substantive finding.

**Escalation check.** This is a genuine project-lead resource decision (Article 21's own domain), made by the project lead directly rather than inferred by the build thread, and is recorded here per CO-022's own rule that nothing may be attributed to the project lead without a verifiable record. No CO-022 escalation category applies — this is the decision itself, not a question requiring one.

### 2026-09-02 — Project-lead action: a second, vendored copy of the *Codex Theodosianus* supplied directly, closing the gap the "Project-lead decision: the *Codex Theodosianus* file supplied this session is referenced, not vendored" entry left open

**Context.** Discussing the "Project-lead decision: the *Codex Theodosianus* file supplied this session is referenced, not vendored" entry's own OTA/CC-BY-NC-SA gap, the project lead asked whether The Latin Library would be a good source for the same work, since its own texts are held out as public domain and organized book by book — meaning acquisition would mean "downloading it chapter by chapter." The build thread's assessment, given directly: The Latin Library's own texts are genuinely public domain by the site's own stated diligence (no restrictive license, unlike the OTA file), but the site does not name which critical edition any given transcription follows and states explicitly that its texts are "not intended for research purposes nor as substitutes for critical editions" — a real concern for a legal corpus where a constitution's exact wording is often the point. The recommendation given: if acquired, vendor it, but disclose the edition gap honestly (Confidence C) rather than treat it as equivalent to a named critical edition, and keep the OTA/Mommsen-Meyer file as the reference standard for anything turning on precise wording — the same pattern already used for the sibling Donatism build's own Boyd 1905 substitute.

**The action.** The project lead then supplied a file directly: all 16 books of the *Codex Theodosianus*, downloaded book by book from The Latin Library (`thelatinlibrary.com/theodosius.html`) and compiled into one document — confirmed as this source by the file's own trailing line, which names its own origin ("Theodosian Code / Ius Romanum / The Latin Library / The Classics Page"), and by the file's own authorship metadata (created 2026-09-02, under the project lead's own name).

**Consequence.** Vendored at `cic/texts/codex-theodosianus_latinlibrary.txt`, with a provenance header stating the rights basis and disclosing the edition-provenance gap plainly, following the recommendation given above rather than treating the two files as interchangeable. `cic/engine/texts_registry.py`'s own `ENTRIES` tuple and `cic/texts/README.md` (regenerated via `--write-readme`) both carry the new file. Registry row 88 records it as a second, Confidence C entry for the same underlying work row 44 already carries by reference only — row 44 itself updated to cross-reference row 88, and `Doc_02_Source_Ecology.md` §5 updated to name both. Neither row is currently drawn on for a specific claim in this document set, so this action changes no substantive finding.

**Escalation check.** This is a further exercise of the same project-lead resource decision the "Project-lead decision: the *Codex Theodosianus* file supplied this session is referenced, not vendored" entry records, this time supplying rather than only ruling on a source — recorded here per the same CO-022 attribution rule. No CO-022 escalation category applies.

### 2026-09-02 — Sixth post-disposition Doc_01 edit (dropping the running count), and this log's own directional-pointer fix, both missing from this log until Round 8 found the gap

**Context.** Doc_02's own Round 7 independent adversarial review found Doc_01's post-disposition edit count wrong for a fourth consecutive round (its own M1) and, separately, found this log's own claim to have retired directional pointers ("above," "below") contradicted in the very entries added alongside that claim (its own M2). Both were fixed in the same commit (`9adbe666`): Doc_01's status line and §9 note were rewritten to state no running count at all, pointing instead to this log as "the authoritative, append-only record"; and the "entry above"/"entry below" language in the two *Codex Theodosianus* entries (titled "Project-lead decision: the *Codex Theodosianus* file supplied this session is referenced, not vendored" and "Project-lead action: a second, vendored copy of the *Codex Theodosianus* supplied directly...") and in the "Doc_01 pointer correction (Round 5 fix pass)" entry's own heading note was rewritten with named-heading pointers. That commit is itself Doc_01's own sixth post-disposition edit (`git log --follow` on Doc_01 now shows six commits after its 2026-09-01 self-disposition) — but no entry recording it was added to this log at the time, even though every one of the five prior post-disposition edits received one. Doc_02's own Round 8 independent adversarial review found the gap (its own M2): Doc_01 now tells a reader this log is the complete record, and until this entry, it was not.

**Consequence.** This entry closes that gap. No further correction to Doc_01 or the Registry's row-52/row-60 placement notes was needed — both were already right; only this log's own disclosure of the edit that made them right was missing.

**Escalation check.** No disposition is reopened — Doc_02, the Registry, and the Manifest remain undisposed drafts; Doc_01 remains Approved to proceed, its own status line's claim about this log now made true by this entry's own existence rather than corrected in Doc_01 itself. No CO-022 category applies. Logged per CO-022's own *Revision decision* rule; a further instance of this document set's own recurring pattern (an assertion about the completeness of its own record, made by the same act that leaves it incomplete), this time in the log's own account of itself rather than in a review artifact or a build-thread claim.

### 2026-09-02 — Doc_02, `Source_Registry.md`, and `Source_Acquisition_Manifest.md` self-disposed to Approved to proceed, after Round 14 independent adversarial review returned no finding at all

**Context.** Fourteen consecutive rounds of independent adversarial review ran against this document set (`Doc_02_Source_Ecology.md`, `Source_Registry.md`, `Source_Acquisition_Manifest.md`), each round's own review artifact filed at `Review-Artifacts/Doc02_RoundN_Review.md`. Rounds 1 through 13 each returned SUBSTANTIAL REVISION REQUIRED and found at least one genuine, previously-uncaught defect — the full findings and fix history for each is in its own review artifact, not restated here. HIGH findings fell monotonically to zero by Round 5 and stayed there for ten consecutive rounds; MEDIUM findings did not fall monotonically but reached single digits by Round 7 and stayed at 0–2 thereafter. No round after Round 5 falsified a historical, bibliographic, or primary-source claim about Cyprian, Augustine, or their sources — every finding from Round 6 onward was a defect in this document set's own record-keeping, cross-document consistency, or Registry-field classification, not a factual error about this world's own ecology. Round 14 (`Review-Artifacts/Doc02_Round14_Review.md`) independently re-derived the entire record rather than trusting Round 13's own account of itself — re-parsing all 177 Registry rows then held, re-running `git log --follow` on Doc_01, recomputing the corpus-map census figures from raw YAML, and re-deriving the Decision Log's own directional-pointer retirement from first principles — and returned **NO SUBSTANTIAL REVISION REQUIRED — CLEARED REVIEW: 0 HIGH, 0 MEDIUM, 0 LOW, 0 COSMETIC**, the first round in this document set's history to return this result.

**Independent re-verification by the build thread, before treating a clean round as sufficient to act on.** Given this project's own standing discipline that a clean result is checked with the same rigor as a dirty one — and this being the first clean round this document set has ever produced — the build thread independently re-verified Round 14's own headline claims a further time, directly against the artifacts rather than against the review's own account of them: the Registry's row count (177 at the time) and full pipe-structure integrity (programmatically re-parsed, zero mismatches, no gaps or duplicates), the current Excluded set (`{28, 29, 98, 128}`, re-parsed from the Boundary Status column directly), Doc_01's six-post-disposition-commit count (re-run via `git log --follow`), and the HIGH/MEDIUM finding sequences (recomputed from all fourteen review artifacts' own stated `Findings:` lines). All held exactly as Round 14 stated.

**Consequence.** `Doc_02_Source_Ecology.md`, `Source_Registry.md`, and `Source_Acquisition_Manifest.md` are self-disposed by the build thread to **Approved to proceed**, per CO-022's own rule that "if no escalation category applies, the build thread applies this itself" — no escalation category applies, confirmed independently by Round 14 and not merely carried forward from any prior round's assessment. This is a lightweight go-ahead, not a claim of completeness: the V7.4 field-bibliography sweep proper remains knowingly unrun, as Doc_02 §10 and the Registry's own saturation statement both disclose. Round 14's own ten-item recall test and three PRESS answers were dispositioned into Registry rows 178–190 in the same pass. `Source_Acquisition_Manifest.md`'s own G1–G9 acquisition candidates are unaffected by this disposition and remain open, awaiting the project lead's own decision at the G1 gate.

**Escalation check.** This is the disposition itself, applied per CO-022's own self-dispositioning rule for procedural work where no escalation category applies — not a question requiring escalation. Logged here per `cic-build-cycle`'s own instruction that a document reaching at least Approved to proceed be logged: document names, review outcome, round count (fourteen), review-artifact location (`Review-Artifacts/Doc02_Round1_Review.md` through `Doc02_Round14_Review.md`), and disposition (Approved to proceed, all three documents, 2026-09-02).

### 2026-09-02 — First three post-disposition `Source_Registry.md` edits: resolving self-flagged bibliographic uncertainty on rows 31, 32, 33 via WebSearch

**Context.** After disposition, the project lead directed that the document set be made "as complete as possible" today while he works separately on G1–G9 source acquisition, and explicitly declined to move forward to Doc_03 in the meantime. With no new files to process yet, the build thread looked for completeness work answerable with tools already available this session (WebSearch) rather than new source material. A search for Registry rows still marked "not independently re-read this session" found five: rows 30, 31, 32, 33, 36 — all in-copyright secondary scholarship (Brown, Lancel, Burns, Burns & Jensen, Shaw), not vendored primary texts, so re-reading `cic/texts/` files against them (the build thread's original plan for this work, recorded before the prior compaction) was not the applicable lever. Of these, rows 31 and 32 already carried a specific, named open question ("flagged for priority second-opinion review on the exact... publication year before it supports any dated claim"); row 33 was Confidence C on an unverified field-knowledge recollection, for a work its own row describes as "the standard modern synthesis... across this world's own full span."

**What was done.** WebSearch (available this session; direct WebFetch to bibliographic-catalogue hosts such as WorldCat remains blocked by the environment's egress policy, consistent with this session's standing network restriction) resolved all three:
- **Row 31** (Lancel, *Saint Augustine*): the previously-flagged English publication year is now confirmed by two independent contemporary journal reviews (*Journal of Ecclesiastical History* and *New Blackfriars*, both via Cambridge Core) citing the identical imprint — SCM Press, London, 2002, trans. Antonia Nevill, ISBN 0 334 02866 3. Translator name added to the citation; flag resolved.
- **Row 32** (Burns, *Cyprian the Bishop*): the "2001 vs. 2002" discrepancy the original WebSearch turned up is a genuine two-edition case, not an error — hardback (ISBN 978-0-415-23849-6, Dec 2001) and paperback (ISBN 978-0-415-23850-2, 2002), independently confirmed via bookseller/catalogue records. This Registry's own citation (2002) matches the paperback; both editions are now recorded. Flag resolved.
- **Row 33** (Burns & Jensen, *Christianity in Roman Africa*): publisher, city, year, ISBN, and collaborator list independently confirmed across five sources (three scholarly-journal reviews, a fourth review hosted on Project MUSE rather than itself a journal, and the publisher's own catalogue page — corrected here, independent review Round 23's own L4, from an earlier draft's own "four scholarly-journal reviews," which called all four instruments journals), all consistent. Row 36 (Shaw) was checked and found to need no update — it already rests on the sibling Donatism build's own independently-verified Registry row, not on an unresolved question of its own.

**Why row 33's Confidence letter was not changed.** Rows 30–32 all carry Confidence B on the pattern "WebSearch-verified bibliographic details, not independently read." Row 33's new verification fits that same pattern and would support the same letter — but a Confidence-rating change is one of the four things CO-022 defines as a substantial revision, and this document set is already disposed (Approved to proceed). The build thread did not self-apply the upgrade. The Verification Note records the new evidence and names the candidate upgrade explicitly, left for the project lead or a future review round to decide rather than silently applied.

**Scope note, for the record.** A broader grep found "flagged for priority second-opinion review" (or equivalent wording) present in roughly half the Registry's rows — the standard caution attached to most WebSearch- or field-knowledge-sourced secondary-literature entries, not a small curated set of special cases. That is an honest disclosure of an inherent epistemic limit (these are, for the most part, in-copyright secondary works the build thread cannot read directly), not itself a defect to resolve row-by-row; resolving all of it is the V7.4 field-bibliography sweep proper, already disclosed elsewhere as knowingly unrun and well beyond today's scope. Today's three rows were chosen because each already named a specific, bounded, checkable question rather than carrying only the generic caution.

**Escalation check.** No CO-022 category applies — this is bibliographic verification-note detail, not a representative-identity decision, a portfolio-level or governance decision, or an unresolved cross-review tension. All three edits are disclosed here per the same post-disposition-edit practice established for `Doc_01_World_Identification.md`. Pipe-structure integrity re-checked programmatically after editing (12 pipes/row on all three edited rows, unchanged from the rest of the table).

### 2026-09-02 — Portfolio-level tangent, logged here for the record only: a cross-world "Library Build Engine" thread commissioned by the project lead

**Not an lpc-ecology decision — flagged as portfolio-level per CO-022's own category, recorded here only because it arose from a conversation inside this world's own thread.** While discussing funding/access options for the in-copyright secondary works named in rows 30–36 (Brown, Lancel, Burns, Burns & Jensen, Shaw), the project lead identified a problem that is not specific to this world: as this project's build sequence reaches later historical periods (he named the Reformation and Enlightenment specifically), proportionally more of the standard scholarly material will sit behind modern copyright, the same "old-PD-edition vs. new-in-copyright-edition of the same underlying ancient text" dynamic this world's own G1/G4 candidates (Hartel's CSEL 3 vs. the modern CCSL 3; Goldbacher's CSEL vs. a hypothetical modern Augustine edition) already surfaced sharply. The project lead directed that a new, standing, cross-world thread be set up — not scoped to any single world's build — to find creative, legitimate (never copyright-evading) ways to build and store a shared library of usable source material feeding all worlds, extending the already-shared `cic/texts/` corpus rather than replacing any world's own per-world Source Registry process.

**Action taken.** A new Claude Code Remote session was created (`session_01CeFxRLYeZxyc5dSb1Xq7Tg`, titled "CiC Library Build Engine — Cross-World Source Acquisition Strategy," tagged `cic-library-engine`), briefed on: this world's own recent findings as evidence for the problem; the existing, superseded `L3B-World-Build-Methodology/Cross_World_Source_Registry.md` (a related but different prior effort — a same-source-claimed-by-two-worlds dedup check, not an acquisition-strategy engine, deferred 2026-07-04 as "separate follow-up work"); `Project-Reference/CiC_Governance_Standing_Rules.md` as the precedent for how adopted cross-cutting rules get recorded; and this project's own numbered Change Order convention, with instructions to propose scope, a home location, and a first deliverable to the project lead before committing to anything binding on other worlds' build threads, rather than acting unilaterally.

**Why this belongs in this log at all.** Nothing about lpc's own disposition, Registry, or Manifest changed as a result — this world's own document set remains Approved to proceed exactly as recorded in the prior entry, and Mark's standing instruction that lpc not move forward to Doc_03 is unaffected. This entry exists only so a future reader of this log isn't left wondering why a cross-world session ID appears in an otherwise per-world record. No CO-022 escalation was required — the project lead commissioned this directly, which is the top of that escalation chain, not a step below it.

### 2026-09-03 — lpc's branch merged with the new shared cross-world library engine (`main`, 158 commits), per the project lead's explicit direction

**Context.** The Library Build Engine thread (`session_01CeFxRLYeZxyc5dSb1Xq7Tg`, logged in the entry above) produced substantial shared infrastructure on `main`: `cic/corpus-map/` (a generated, per-work index of which vendored texts serve which Atlas/census entry, non-exclusive by role — tradition/context/antecedent/transmission), `cic/texts/REGISTRY.yaml` + `INTAKE.md` (replacing `texts_registry.py`'s old hardcoded `ENTRIES` tuple with a YAML-backed registry and a formal intake procedure, including a rights checklist for original-language "second witness" sourcing), and Cappadocian's admission as the 7th built world. The project lead directed (in chat, this session, 2026-09-03) that lpc's branch adopt this system, calling it likely to produce a better build; asked what concerns this raised; and then directed the migration proceed, starting with a dry-run merge before anything real.

**Dry-run merge, performed first, on a throwaway branch (`dry-run-main-merge-lpc`, deleted after inspection, never touching the real branch).** Confirmed: of 535 files the merge touches, only two conflict, both from lpc's own prior Codex Theodosianus vendoring work (`cic/engine/texts_registry.py`, `cic/texts/README.md`); nothing under this world's own `World-Builds/Latin-Pastoral-Congregational-Christianity/` folder is touched at all by anything in main's 158 commits — the disposed Doc_02/Registry/Manifest/this log were never at risk. Reported this to the project lead before doing the real merge.

**Real merge, performed after project-lead go-ahead.** `git merge origin/main` on the real branch. Resolved the two conflicts:
- `cic/engine/texts_registry.py`: took main's refactored version wholesale (loads `ENTRIES` from `REGISTRY.yaml` now, not a hardcoded tuple) — verified first, by direct comparison, that every one of the 47 entries in lpc branch's old hardcoded tuple already exists in main's 65-row `REGISTRY.yaml`, except one: `codex-theodosianus_latinlibrary.txt`, lpc's own prior addition.
- `cic/texts/REGISTRY.yaml`: added that one missing row, carrying over the exact provenance text originally written for the old tuple entry, in the new schema.
- `cic/texts/README.md`: regenerated fresh via `python cic/engine/texts_registry.py --write-readme` rather than hand-merged, since it's a generated file.

**A real gap the merge surfaced and fixed.** `codex-theodosianus_latinlibrary.txt`'s own header predates the new intake-header convention (`cic/texts/INTAKE.md` §4) and had no `Language:` line — meaning `texts_registry.py`'s `report()` was silently treating 1.78MB of Latin text as English (the script's own documented default: absence of a `Language:` line means English). Added `Language: lat` to the file's own header. Post-fix, `python cic/engine/texts_registry.py` correctly lists it under "non-English source(s)" alongside `nestle1904_greek-new-testament-grc.txt`, and the full validation sweep passes clean (66 vendored files, all rights-verified, no orphans, no dangling registry rows).

**A tangential, useful discovery, not acted on.** While investigating an unexpected `M` flag on the vendored file during the merge, found (and ruled out as unrelated) a Cappadocian-world source record, `records/cappadocian/source/cappadocian.source.codex-theodosianus-13-3-5-school-law.md`, independently citing *Codex Theodosianus* 13.3.5 (Julian's 362 CE school law) as "not yet acquired as an open text." Confirmed lpc's own vendored file genuinely contains that section (line 6031). Not lpc's record to edit — flagged here for the record and for a future relay to the Library Engine thread, not acted on unilaterally.

**Verified before and after committing:** `git status --short` before commit showed nothing under this world's own folder touched; `python cic/engine/texts_registry.py` passed clean post-merge; the merge commit (`bef08614`) was pushed to `origin/claude/record-native-world-build-v2-yq11wl`.

**What this does NOT do.** This merge brings the shared library infrastructure onto lpc's branch. It does not migrate `Source_Registry.md`'s own 190 rows into any new format, does not run the corpus-map cross-check against the pre-generated `cic/corpus-map/latin-pastoral-congregational-christianity.yaml` (73 works, role: tradition), does not stage the Codex Theodosianus file into corpus-map assignment, and does not touch the still-open G1–G9 candidates or `download-queue-seed.yaml`. Those remain the next steps of the migration process discussed with the project lead this session, not yet begun.

**Escalation check.** No CO-022 category applies on its own terms — this is neither a representative-identity decision, a portfolio-level decision made unilaterally (the project lead directed it explicitly, twice, after being shown the concerns), nor an unresolved cross-review tension. Whether the eventual corpus-map cross-check surfaces new Registry rows requiring a fresh review round remains an open question, to be assessed once that step actually runs, not decided in advance here.

### 2026-09-03 — Corpus-map cross-check run: clean result, zero new rows, and confirmation this check was already done once before

**What was checked.** Per the project lead's direction to proceed with the migration process's next step, the build thread cross-checked `cic/corpus-map/latin-pastoral-congregational-christianity.yaml` (the shared, generated, per-work assignment index — 73 works, all `role: tradition`, spanning `anf05`, `anf09`, `npnf101`–`npnf108`, `npnf214`, and `optatus_against-the-donatists.txt`) against `Source_Registry.md`'s full 190-row table, work by work.

**Result: complete coverage, zero gaps.** Every one of the 73 corpus-map works has a matching Registry row — most inside the Registry's own compound/grouped rows (rows 5, 22, 23, 25 each bundle several related treatises under one row, per this Registry's own established granularity convention). No new row was needed.

**A discovery, not a coincidence: this exact check already happened once, inside the original review cycle.** Row 18's own note ("added here — the one work in the 73-entry census with no prior row, caught this revision") and row 23's own note ("caught by Round 2's own full 73-work/48-row cross-check") both directly describe an earlier pass against what was evidently an earlier state of this same 73-work census, run during Round 2 of the original 14-round review cycle, back when the Registry held only 48 rows. Row 29 (Tertullian, Excluded/Named Comparandum) independently corroborates this: its own Discovery channel column reads "corpus map cross-check / Doc_01 §2, §6, §7 / 2026-09-01" — a citation to that same earlier check. Today's cross-check against the freshly-regenerated (2026-09-02+ cross-world assignment pass) version of the same file confirms that Round 2's work held completely: nothing drifted, nothing was missed, across everything that's changed in the Registry since (48 rows → 190).

**One asymmetry noted, and confirmed correct rather than a gap.** None of the 73 works touch Tertullian's corpus (`anf03`/`anf04`), even though Registry row 29 discusses Tertullian at length. This is not a miss: row 29 itself excludes Tertullian as a Named Comparandum (Doc_01 §2/§6/§7's own "credited with forging this world's own vocabulary, not speaking as this world's own voice"), and correctly points to Tertullian's own separate Atlas bucket (`cic/corpus-map/tertullian-s-voice.yaml`) rather than claiming him as lpc's own tradition. The corpus-map's own zero assignment for Tertullian-to-lpc is exactly what row 29's own exclusion predicts.

**What this step did not do.** Did not stage the Codex Theodosianus file into corpus-map assignment (still pending — a `_staging/` entry + `corpus_map_merge.py --write-only`), did not port the open G1–G9 candidates into `world-build-docs/_cross-world/download-queue-seed.yaml`, and did not touch canonical-address (`sources[].address`) enrichment, which is a Phase B record-authoring field this Doc_02-stage Registry doesn't carry.

**Escalation check.** No CO-022 category applies — this is a verification pass that confirmed the existing Registry, made no substantive change, and added no new claim. Nothing here is cosmetic-or-substantial in CO-022's sense because nothing was revised at all; recorded per the same "review rounds exist as files, not claims" discipline that governs every other check in this build, so a clean result is on the record with the same rigor as a dirty one would have been.

### 2026-09-03 — Migration completed: Codex Theodosianus staged into corpus-map; six verified G-candidates ported to the shared download queue

**Codex Theodosianus staging.** Wrote `cic/corpus-map/_staging/codex-theodosianus_latinlibrary.yaml`, completing the intake this build thread's own prior vendoring (Registry row 88) never finished under the old system. Per `cic/texts/INTAKE.md`'s "check every world it could serve, not just the one asked for" rule, checked beyond lpc's own Atlas entry before writing it:
- **lpc:** role `context`, confidence `provisional` — the imperial statute behind the Theodosian-law fine Augustine's own Letter 185 §25 cites (row 44/row 12), not yet drawn on directly for a specific claim.
- **donatism:** role `context`, confidence `needs-ruling` — flagged, not asserted, because Registry row 44 already records that build's own prior G3 request for this exact edition (five failed acquisition attempts, settled for a substitute); its own corpus-map bucket had no Codex Theodosianus entry before this.
- **imperial-juridical-christianity:** role `context`, confidence `needs-ruling` — named on thematic fit alone; explicitly not asserted as `tradition` vs. `context` since this staging entry has no standing to read that world's own Doc_01 boundary from outside its own build thread.

A new author entity, `theodosian-code-compilers` (collective/anonymous), was ruled in `UNATTRIBUTED.yaml`. Validated via `corpus_map_merge.py --check` first, written via `corpus_map_merge.py --write-only codex-theodosianus_latinlibrary` (scoped — no other in-flight staging work touched or pruned), then `corpus_index.py --build` re-run so the file is actually searchable now.

I–III), G7 (von Soden's *Briefsammlung*), and G9 (Delehaye) — each already carrying a real, independently-verified archive.org identifier in this world's own Manifest, meeting the shared queue's own "verified against a real host" bar. **G2, G6, and G8 were deliberately left out** — none has a located identifier in lpc's own Manifest (an already-disclosed gap, not a placeholder), so none meets that bar. `gen_download_queue.py` re-run to regenerate `DOWNLOAD-QUEUE.md` from the updated seed.

**What this does and does not change.** These G-candidates remain open, unacquired, and awaiting the project lead's own G1-gate decision exactly as before — porting them only makes them visible to other worlds' build threads too, per the shared queue's own stated purpose ("a source found once should never need finding twice"). Nothing in `Source_Acquisition_Manifest.md` itself changed as a result; it remains the authoritative account of lpc's own reasoning for each candidate.

**This completes the migration process laid out earlier this session:** dry-run merge → real merge → corpus-map cross-check (prior entry, clean, zero gaps) → this entry's two remaining steps. lpc's branch is now current with the shared library engine on `main` as of commit `56a709e8`.

**Escalation check.** No CO-022 category applies. The `needs-ruling` confidence flags for donatism and imperial-juridical-christianity are deliberately non-committal — they name a candidate for another world's own build thread to decide, not a decision made on that thread's behalf.

### 2026-09-05 — G1 (Hartel's Cyprian, CSEL 3) fulfilled for Pars I–II: intake, registry, corpus-map, and Registry row 191

**Context.** After the CiC pilot's launch (7 worlds live, lpc and Donatism as 8 and 9), the project lead asked this build thread for a specific, linked download list. This thread supplied one (G1, G3, G4, G5, G7, G9 with real archive.org identifiers; G2, G6, G8 disclosed as still unlocated after a fresh search attempt, including two genuinely new but unconfirmed leads — a HathiTrust catalog record for Hartel's edition and confirmation that HathiTrust hosts the CSEL series broadly, neither of which this session could open directly to verify, both flagged as leads rather than confirmed identifiers). The project lead then supplied a compiled docx: `S._Thasci_Caecili_Cypriani_Opera_omnia.docx`.

**What was actually received, verified before anything else per `cic/texts/INTAKE.md` §1.** The docx is a copy of an archive.org "Full text" / "See other formats" view of a Google Books scan of the 1965 Johnson Reprint Corporation photomechanical reprint of Hartel's edition — confirmed from the file's own embedded content (Google's standard public-domain usage notice, the archive.org page's own navigation chrome, both present at the head of what was supplied). The exact archive.org item identifier was not stated by the project lead and was not guessed, per this project's own no-invented-identifiers discipline — left as an open item in the file's own header and in the Registry row below at the time this entry was written. **Corrected here, independent review Round 25's own M2, from an earlier draft of this sentence that still described both as open: the identifier was independently confirmed later, independent review Round 24's own M3 (`sthascicaecilic01hartgoog`), and both the file's own header and Registry row 191 now say so, matching the treatment the sibling G3 entry below already carries.**

**Scope discovered, not assumed.** The title page read "VOL. III. PARS I." (the treatises, 1868); a second title page at roughly the document's midpoint read "VOL. III. PARS II." (dated 1871, the *Epistulae*), confirmed by paragraph-index search for volume/part markers across the full 30,932-paragraph document rather than inferred from the title alone. The document runs Epistulae I through LXXXI and ends cleanly (no truncation found, checked directly against the document's own last paragraphs) at the close of Epistle 81's own apparatus notes. This is a substantially more complete acquisition than the single vol.-3.2-only archive.org item this Manifest had previously located — Pars I and Pars II together, not Pars II alone. Pars III (spuria and indices) is not present and was not claimed to be.

**OCR quality assessed directly, not assumed, given a recent precedent for rejecting a bad scan.** Sampled at six points spanning the full document (the opening treatise, ~25%, ~50%/the Pars I-II transition, ~75%, and the closing paragraphs) rather than only the beginning. Finding: the running Latin body text reads coherently throughout every sample, including at the very end; the apparatus criticus (manuscript sigla, textual variants) carries real, meaningfully worse OCR noise — garbled sigla, stray characters — than the running text. This is a narrower defect than the Tischendorf scan the cross-world Library Engine thread rejected this same week (where the Latin title page itself was unreadable and the apparatus was called "unusable noise"): here both title pages read correctly, and the apparatus, while noisy, is not unusable, only unreliable for a claim resting on a specific manuscript reading. Disclosed as such in the file's own header and in the Registry rather than treated as pristine or silently accepted.

**Action taken, following the same intake procedure used for the Codex Theodosianus and Nestle 1904 files this project has already established:**
- Extracted mechanically from the docx (site-navigation chrome and Google's own boilerplate usage notice cut; doubled/tripled inter-word spacing — an artifact of the source page's own text export — normalized to single spaces; nothing else altered, no OCR error corrected, no apparatus reformatted).
- Vendored at `cic/texts/cyprian_opera-omnia-critical_hartel-csel3-pars1-2.txt`, with a full intake header per `cic/texts/INTAKE.md` §4.
- Registered in `cic/texts/REGISTRY.yaml`; `python cic/engine/texts_registry.py` validates clean (67 vendored files, all rights-verified, no orphans); `--write-readme` and `corpus_index.py --build` re-run.
- Staged into corpus-map (`_staging/cyprian_opera-omnia-critical_hartel-csel3-pars1-2.yaml`), one combined `role: tradition`, `confidence: assigned` entry for `latin-pastoral-congregational-christianity` — the Latin original behind the already-vendored ANF05 English translation's own Cyprian works, per this map's own granularity rule (one entry for a large multi-part body, not one per internal division, matching how the Psalms and Tractates-on-John bodies are already handled). Validated via `--check`, written via `--write-only cyprian_opera-omnia-critical_hartel-csel3-pars1-2` (scoped, no pruning of concurrent staging work).
- `Source_Registry.md`: added row 191 documenting the vendored file, its OCR-quality assessment, and the open Pars III gap; row 39 (the standing reference-edition row) updated to point to it, following the same pattern row 44 → row 88 already established for the Codex Theodosianus.
- `Source_Acquisition_Manifest.md`'s own G1 entry updated in place with a dated fulfillment note, Pars III left explicitly open.

### 2026-09-05 — G3 (Possidius's *Life of Augustine*, Weiskotten 1919) fulfilled: intake, registry, corpus-map, and Registry row 192

**Context.** Immediately following G1's fulfillment, the project lead asked this build thread to fetch G3 directly. Network access to archive.org was re-checked and re-confirmed blocked at the infrastructure level — this time verified two ways (the `WebFetch` tool and a direct `curl`, both against the same host, both rejected identically), with the proxy's own status log showing the exact cause: a `403` policy denial at the gateway for `archive.org:443`, not a transient or tool-specific failure. Reported plainly rather than worked around. The project lead then downloaded and supplied the file himself, the same pattern as G1.

**Duplicate upload caught before any wasted work.** A second upload arrived shortly after the first, same filename. Checked by hash (`md5sum`) before assuming anything — byte-identical to the first upload. Reported as a duplicate rather than reprocessed.

**What was actually received, verified per `cic/texts/INTAKE.md` §1.** Confirmed via the file's own embedded content: an archive.org "Full text" view of a scan of the University of California, Berkeley's own library copy (a scanned circulation due-date slip bound into the back of the physical volume names the library and its own loan-period policy). Title page confirms this is genuinely Weiskotten's 1919 Princeton dissertation — Latin critical text, introduction, notes, and a complete English translation, exactly the edition this Manifest's G3 named. The exact archive.org item identifier was not stated when supplied and was not guessed at the time. **Corrected here, independent review Round 22's own M1 and M2: the identifier is now independently confirmed — `sanctiaugustiniv00possrich`, matched byte-for-byte at both ends against the vendored file — and this entry's own original "Google Books scan" characterization is wrong: that item's own metadata (sponsor: MSN, scanningcenter: rich) is the signature of an Internet Archive/Microsoft Open Content Alliance scan, and its own full text contains the word "Google" zero times.**

**A classification question worth getting right, not assumed either way.** Unlike the Codex Theodosianus and Hartel files (Latin-only, registered as second-witness original-language sources per `cic/texts/INTAKE.md`'s own caveat), this edition carries a complete English translation throughout. Checked directly by sampling the document at multiple points rather than assumed from the title alone — the English reads as real, continuous, coherent translated prose, not a fragment or summary, ending at Possidius's own closing epilogue. Registered accordingly as ordinary English-language primary-evidence material (Confidence A), not second-witness-only — a materially different, more valuable classification than either prior original-language intake this session.

**A second scanning artifact caught by checking the document's own end, not just its beginning.** The same discipline applied to the Hartel file — verifying the file's own true end, not assuming a clean stop — caught a second kind of cruft this time: a scanned UC Berkeley circulation-desk due-date card bound into the back of the physical book, entirely unrelated to Possidius's or Weiskotten's own content (loan periods, a phone number, a library form number). Stripped as a scanning artifact, disclosed in the file's own header, the same treatment as the archive.org site-navigation chrome at the front (corrected here, independent review Round 23's own M2, from an earlier draft's own "archive.org/Google Books chrome," a further surviving instance of the M2 correction above, inside this same entry). A first extraction pass also let one stray line (the archive.org page's own title line, which itself contains the words "Sancti Augustini" and so matched a naive title-page search) slip past the front cut; caught and fixed before the file was finalized.

**OCR quality assessed directly, not assumed.** Sampled at multiple points spanning the full ~6,300-paragraph document. The English translation reads cleanly and coherently throughout, including its own final lines. The Latin apparatus criticus and the introduction's manuscript-collation tables carry more noise (garbled sigla, damaged tabular data) — disclosed in the file's own header rather than treated as pristine, the same standard already applied to the Hartel file.

**Action taken, following the same intake procedure now established for the third time this session:**
- Extracted mechanically (archive.org site-navigation chrome and the library due-date slip cut — not "Google Books chrome," per the M2 correction above; the one stray leaked line fixed; doubled inter-word spacing normalized; nothing else altered).
- Vendored at `cic/texts/possidius_vita-augustini_weiskotten1919.txt`, with a full intake header.
- Registered in `cic/texts/REGISTRY.yaml`; `python cic/engine/texts_registry.py` validates clean (68 vendored files); `--write-readme` and `corpus_index.py --build` re-run.
- Staged into corpus-map, `role: tradition`, `confidence: assigned`, for `latin-pastoral-congregational-christianity` only (no cross-world candidacy argued here, unlike the Codex Theodosianus file — this is squarely lpc's own Augustine-side formation-narrative material). Validated via `--check`, written via `--write-only possidius_vita-augustini_weiskotten1919`.
- `Source_Registry.md`: added row 192 (Confidence A, per the classification above) documenting the vendored file and its OCR-quality assessment; row 45 updated to point to it, following the same row-39→191 and row-44→88 pattern.
- `Source_Acquisition_Manifest.md`'s own G3 entry updated in place with a dated fulfillment note — unlike G1, **G3 closes in full**, no remaining part of the request stays open.
- `world-build-docs/_cross-world/download-queue-seed.yaml`'s existing G3 entry updated to `status: downloaded` in place (no superseding entry needed, since the one entry already named exactly this edition and this acquisition fulfills it completely).
- **Flagged, not made unilaterally:** Doc_01 §5's and §9's own disclosure that the Megalius identification rests on "an unvendored work" is now stale — row 192's own Verification Note flags this for whoever next touches Doc_01, rather than this row editing that document itself.

**What remains open.** The exact archive.org identifier is no longer open — resolved above, independent review Round 22's own M1 (`sanctiaugustiniv00possrich`), corrected here from an earlier draft of this entry that still listed it as outstanding. Doc_01's own stale "unvendored" disclosure, flagged above, not yet corrected. The same standing cross-world-file asymmetry noted in the prior entry (this branch's own contributions to `cic/corpus-map/` and `world-build-docs/_cross-world/` remain unmerged into `main`) continues to apply, now compounded by a further intake since that note was written.

**Escalation check.** No CO-022 category applies — a routine, disclosed intake of a project-lead-supplied file, following the same established procedure a third time. ### 2026-09-05 — G4 partially fulfilled: Goldbacher's Augustine Epistulae, CSEL 57 Pars IV (Epistulae 185–270); Registry row 193

**Context.** Immediately following G3, the project lead supplied a compiled docx of Goldbacher's critical edition. Confirmed from the title page: CSEL 57, Pars IV, Epistulae CLXXXV–CCLXX (185–270) — one of the five volumes (34/1, 34/2, 44, 57, 58) G4 names collectively, matching the archive.org item this Manifest had already identified (`archive.org/details/CSEL57`) — **independently confirmed, not merely matched, corrected here, independent review Round 24's own L1 (corrected here, Round 25's own L3, from an earlier draft's own "L1 and M1," which cited a finding — Round 24's M1, this same entry's own action list — unrelated to this identifier), from an earlier draft's own "not independently confirmed": the item's own full text matches this particular copy character for character at both ends.**

**OCR quality quantified this time, not just sampled qualitatively.** Sampling across the ~24,350-paragraph document found the same pattern as the two prior Latin intakes — clean running letter text, noisier apparatus — but at a point sharp enough to check with numbers rather than impressions alone: a direct paragraph-level scan for heavy garbage-symbol corruption (regex-matched runs of `*^<>%™#@` and similar) found **636 of 24,350 paragraphs (≈2.6%)** affected, concentrated in the apparatus criticus and editorial notes. At least one specific editorial note (on a letter's own place in the transmitted Hieronymian correspondence) was checked directly and found essentially unreadable, not merely noisy — worse than anything found in the Hartel or Possidius intakes. The running letter text itself, checked at multiple points including the collection's own final lines, was unaffected. This is disclosed with the actual measured rate in both the file's own header and Registry row 193, rather than described only in qualitative terms as the prior two rows were — a more rigorous standard than this session's own earlier practice, adopted because the defect here genuinely warranted it, not applied retroactively to the earlier rows.

**Classification.** Latin-only, no facing translation (unlike Possidius) — registered as an original-language second-witness per `cic/texts/INTAKE.md`, the same footing as the Hartel and Codex Theodosianus files, not the Confidence-A ordinary-evidence footing Possidius received.

**A schema-conformance error caught and fixed before committing.** A first attempt recorded the shared `download-queue-seed.yaml`'s own status field as `partially-downloaded` — not one of that file's own three documented values (`not-yet-downloaded | downloaded | superseded`). Caught by checking the schema directly rather than assuming the value would validate. Fixed by splitting the single five-volume entry into two: one narrowed to the four still-open volumes (34/1, 34/2, 44, 58) at `not-yet-downloaded`, and a new one for the CSEL 57 acquisition at `downloaded` — the same underlying idea `partially-downloaded` was reaching for, expressed in terms the schema actually supports, following this queue's own append-only discipline (the original entry narrowed and kept, not deleted).

**Action taken:**
- Extracted mechanically (archive.org site-navigation chrome cut — not "Google Books chrome," per the M2 correction above (corrected here, independent review Round 24's own M1, a further surviving instance of that same correction, in this entry's own action list); no trailing library-artifact this time, checked directly against the document's own true end; doubled inter-word spacing normalized; nothing else altered).
- Vendored at `cic/texts/augustine_epistulae-critical_goldbacher-csel57-pars4.txt`, with a full intake header including the quantified OCR finding.
- Registered in `cic/texts/REGISTRY.yaml`; `python cic/engine/texts_registry.py` validates clean (69 vendored files); `--write-readme` and `corpus_index.py --build` re-run.
- Staged into corpus-map, `role: tradition`, `confidence: assigned`, for `latin-pastoral-congregational-christianity` — the Latin original of the same letter range already assigned from the vendored NPNF translation, including Letter 185 itself (Registry row 12). Validated via `--check`, written via `--write-only augustine_epistulae-critical_goldbacher-csel57-pars4`.
