### 2026-09-08 — Ten sources independently vendored under this thread's own convention, closing a prior sibling research session's work

**Context.** A separate, prior research session (session_01WLxhNbVhjkf1R2SAh8dxxT, branch `claude/latin-pastoral-source-integration`, built off `main`, never merged anywhere) had, with its own working network access, located and vendored ten Latin/Greek/French/German sources bearing on this world — none of it merged, none of it on this branch, and per this build's own standing discipline, none of it to be trusted without independent re-verification. Five Fable-model research agents (per this session's own model assignment for research/verification work) were dispatched in parallel, each independently fetching the live archive.org text for a batch of the ten items and quoting exact confirmatory passages, rather than trusting the sibling session's own characterization of any of them.

**What was independently re-verified, and what it found.** All ten claims held on their own facts — real archive.org identifiers, genuine title pages, genuine content — but re-verification surfaced two errors and one significant unresolved question the sibling session's own record either got wrong or left unsettled:
1. **A real extraction hazard the sibling session's own file may or may not have avoided, checked directly rather than assumed either way.** Hartel's CSEL 3 Pars III (`corpusscriptorum03cypruoft`) is bound in the same archive.org scan with CSEL 4 (Arnobius, *Adversus Nationes*) — an entirely unrelated work beginning immediately after Pars III's own content ends. This build thread's own file (row 194) was independently fetched and the Arnobius material excised at the correct boundary, confirmed absent by a post-extraction grep; the sibling session's own separately-vendored file for the same underlying content was not inspected for the same defect, since this build thread built its own file from a fresh fetch rather than adopting the sibling's copy.
2. **A misleading filename on the sibling session's own vendored file, independently investigated rather than taken at face value.** The sibling session named its own vendoring of `archive.org/details/chronicaminorasa09momm` `chronica-minora-liber-genealogus_mommsen1892.txt` — a name that, read plainly, implies the file's target content is the *Liber Genealogus* rather than Prosper of Aquitaine's own *Epitoma Chronicon*, this world's own actual target text within that volume (Mommsen's *Chronica Minora* binds several distinct chronicles together). Independently checked: the sibling file's own *content* is correct — it carries Prosper's text in full, and its own header and Registry note correctly identify the target — only the filename was misleading. This build thread's own file (row 203) is independently fetched and named for clarity (`prosper_chronica-minora-1-lat_mommsen1892.txt`) rather than adopted from the sibling's copy or its name corrected in place on a branch this thread does not own.
3. **A characterization in this session's own task brief, independently checked and found wrong.** The brief describing this work characterized Perpetua's own Passion (present in the Robinson 1891 volume alongside the Scillitan Martyrs) as "not yet assigned to any world's corpus map." Checked directly against `cic/corpus-map/tertullian-s-voice.yaml` before writing anything: it is already assigned there, "RULED BY MARK, 2026-08-26." This build thread's own new Latin/Greek file (row 204) mirrors that existing ruling rather than reassigning the work, and this entry corrects the brief's own claim rather than silently reproducing it — the same standing discipline that has caught an unre-derived claim reaching a live document at every prior stage of this build.

**Action taken.** All ten items independently re-fetched directly from archive.org (not copied from the sibling branch), re-vendored under this thread's own `cic/texts/INTAKE.md` header convention (Title/Creator/Publisher/Publication Date/Rights/Language/Source/Provenance note/Content note — distinct from the sibling session's own slightly different convention), registered in `cic/texts/REGISTRY.yaml` (rows now 88 total, up from 69), staged into the corpus-map (`_staging/` files, validated via `--check` before merge, merged clean), and logged as Source_Registry.md rows 194 and 197–204 (nine rows, one file each; row 195/196 cover a tenth and eleventh file from this same batch — corrected here, independent review of the companion Doc_02 revision, Round 15's own L5, from an earlier draft's miscount of "an eleventh and twelfth" — see the next entry). `python cic/engine/texts_registry.py` validates clean; `--write-readme`, `corpus_authors.py --write`, and `corpus_index.py --build` all re-run.

**Escalation check.** No CO-022 category applies — routine, disclosed vendoring following this world's own established intake procedure, extended to sources a sibling session found first but this thread independently re-verified rather than trusted. The two corrections found (the Arnobius boundary, the misleading filename) and the one brief-correction (Perpetua's own prior assignment) are each disclosed at their own site (this entry, and Registry rows 194/203/204's own Verification Notes) rather than silently absorbed.

### 2026-09-08 — G1 closed in full; G4 narrowed to one small residual; G2, G5, G6, G7, G8, and G9 all closed

**Context.** With network access confirmed working (entry above), this build thread chased the Manifest's own six genuinely-open gaps directly — G2 (Harnack's *Vita Cypriani*), G5 (Monceaux vols. I–III), G6 (Augustine's *Retractationes*, CSEL 36), G7 (von Soden's *Briefsammlung*), G8 (von Soden's *Prosopographie*), G9 (Delehaye's *Passions*) — plus G1's own still-open Pars III and G4's own four still-unacquired Goldbacher volumes. No identifier named below was invented or guessed — every one was actually fetched and read, the same standard this Manifest has applied to G1/G3/G4/G5/G7/G9's own prior leads throughout.

**What closed, and what a fresh search found that no prior round ever had.** G1 (Pars III, `corpusscriptorum03cypruoft`), G4 (CSEL 34/1–2 and CSEL 44, `sanctiaureliaugu34augu` and `sanctiaureliaugu44augu`), G7 (`diecyprianische00unkngoog`), and G9 (`lespassionsdesm00dele`) all fetched directly from identifiers this Manifest had already named but never itself opened. **G2, G6, and G8 — the three items no prior round had ever located an identifier for at all — are now closed on genuinely new finds:** G2 at `texteunduntersuc3839akad` (Harnack's own Heft 3, bound inside *Texte und Untersuchungen* 38–39 — found by searching the TU series directly rather than for "Harnack Cyprian" alone); G6 at `sanctiaureliaugu36augu` (found by opening Getty-hosted CSEL-Augustine volumes directly by number, since their own archive.org metadata carries no per-volume title/editor/year — the reason keyword search alone had repeatedly failed every prior round); G8 at `quellenundforsch12deutuoft` (found by searching for the *serial's* own title, "Quellen und Forschungen aus italienischen Archiven und Bibliotheken," rather than the short article's own title — a short piece bound inside a periodical volume is not independently discoverable any other way).

**A correction to this Manifest's own G5 lead, found by independently opening it rather than trusting it.** G5's own long-standing lead for vol. I, `archive.org/details/histoirelittra00moncuoft`, was independently opened this session and found to be **Tome Cinquième** (1920, *Saint Optat et les premiers écrivains donatistes*) — already vendored on the sibling Donatism build's own branch — not Tome Premier at all. A fresh search located and independently verified the actual three volumes: Tome Premier (`histoirelittra01moncuoft`, 1901), Tome Deuxième (`histoirelittra02moncuoft`, 1902, "Saint Cyprien et son temps" — the volume this Manifest's own G5 names as most directly relevant), and Tome Troisième (`histoirelitterai03monc_0`, 1905), the last also independently corroborated against the Bibliothèque nationale de France's own SRU catalogue record for the Gallica mirror this Manifest had already named as a lead.

**Action taken.** All seven newly-fetched or newly-closed items vendored under this thread's own `cic/texts/INTAKE.md` convention, registered, staged into the corpus-map (validated clean via `--check`, merged), and logged as Source_Registry.md rows 195, 196, 205–212. `Source_Acquisition_Manifest.md`'s own G1, G2, G4, G5, G6, G7, G8, and G9 entries each updated in place with a dated fulfillment paragraph; the §1 network-access-limitation paragraph and the closing "Decision from the project lead" section both given superseding status notes rather than rewritten, per this Manifest's own standing practice of keeping a stale paragraph as historical record while disclosing plainly that it no longer holds. `world-build-docs/_cross-world/download-queue-seed.yaml` updated to match (new `downloaded` entries; the wrong G5 vol.-I entry marked `superseded`, not deleted, per that file's own append-only discipline), `gen_download_queue.py` re-run.

**What remains open.** Only G4's own CSEL 58 (praefatio and indices, no letter text of its own) — a minor, low-value residual not chased further this session after the higher-value items were prioritized. Every other numbered request in this Manifest (G1, G2, G3, G5, G6, G7, G8, G9) is now closed.

**Escalation check.** No CO-022 category applies — each fulfillment is a routine, disclosed intake of a real, independently-verified public-domain source, following this world's own established procedure for the ninth and further times this session. The G5 lead correction is a citation-accuracy fix (the wrong volume named for a still-genuinely-sought work), not a reopening of any finding, conclusion, or disposition — Doc_02, the Registry, and the Manifest's own prior disposition are unaffected in substance, consistent with the same *Coach verification* exception this log has applied to comparable corrections throughout (see, e.g., the 2026-09-01 Epistle XXXIX/XL entry above). Logged here per CO-022's own *Naming and term propagation* rule.

### 2026-09-08 — Doc_02 revision: independent adversarial review rounds against the 2026-09-08 source-integration revision, one dated paragraph appended per round, each finding real defects, most fixed (heading renamed here, independent review Round 21's own L3, from "Rounds 15 through 20," a per-round enumeration this same paragraph's own findings elsewhere had just retired for going stale twice — corrected here rather than repeating that defect a third time — and from "each finding real defects, each fixed," which two rounds' own paragraphs below already record as not true of every finding; corrected a second time, independent review Round 22's own C1, from an earlier draft's own "most finding real defects," which over-hedged the first limb — the pattern paragraph below states plainly that every single round found a real defect — when only the second limb needed hedging; heading renamed once already, independent review Round 20's own M4, from "Rounds 15 and 16: two independent adversarial review rounds," which the Round 17, 18, 19, and 20 fix passes each appended a further round's own paragraph to without renaming)

**Context.** The `Doc_02_Source_Ecology.md` revision folding in the day's own vendoring (the two entries above, and the two before them) was sent through this world's own established review discipline — independent adversarial review on Opus, per this session's own model assignment — rather than self-certified. This entry records what should have been logged at the time each fix pass was made; its own absence until now was itself a finding (Round 16's own M5, below).

**Round 15** (`Review-Artifacts/Doc02_Round15_Review.md`) returned SUBSTANTIAL REVISION REQUIRED — 4 HIGH, 6 MEDIUM, 5 LOW, 2 COSMETIC. The underlying vendoring was found sound (all 19 files present, correctly headed, the flagged Cyprian/Arnobius extraction hazard genuinely absent); the findings were almost entirely about how Doc_02 and `Source_Registry.md` described that work: a self-contradicting row-16/row-200 claim in §2 (H1); a false "added this revision" / "English base unchanged" claim understating that the 73→99 census growth spanned five passes (four before this revision, one on the day itself), not one, and that Possidius's own bilingual edition (row 192) had already changed the English base (H2); the raw 99-entry census count offered as a "primary-source base" figure without disclosing that nine of the ninety-nine are `role: context`, two are exact-title duplicates, and roughly a further nine are second witnesses to already-counted works (H3); seven Registry rows (40, 41, 56, 78, 89, 90, 99) still reading "Not currently vendored" for work vendored that same day, including row 78, which §2 cited as proof of the opposite (H4); a misattributed characterization of Doc_01 §2 and a Hippo/Carthage conflation in the new Prosper paragraph (M1); rows 200/201 wrongly called "critical editions" against their own explicit disclaimer (M2); a "single partial exception" claim false under either reading of its own reference date (M3); an overbroad "cross-checking an English rendering" claim false for the Retractationes and the Acta Proconsularia (M4); Possidius's own Vita — vendored specifically to close a named formation-narrative gap — absent from §1, §2, and §4 despite the gap being the acquisition's own stated purpose (M5); and Harnack missing from §3's secondary-scholarship count (M6). Of the seventeen findings Round 15 made, 16 (H1–H4, M1–M6, L1–L5, C1) were fixed in place; the seventeenth, C2 (a `Language:` field narrower than two files' own mixed-language content), was deliberately left, since Round 15 itself judged it affects no claim and the underlying schema supports only one ISO 639-3 code per file — a real, disclosed constraint, not an oversight, though Round 16 later found this non-fix itself undisclosed anywhere (see below).

**Round 16** (`Review-Artifacts/Doc02_Round16_Review.md`), commissioned specifically to verify the Round 15 fix pass rather than re-review the whole document, found most of the sixteen applied fixes held — several exactly, including H1's corrected row list (checked against every row, not just the one named) and the full H2/H3 census arithmetic (independently recomputed and confirmed to the digit: 99 total, 97 distinct titles, 90 `tradition`/9 `context`, 22 added that day). But it found four HIGH-severity regressions the fix pass itself introduced: **M2 was fixed at §1 and not at §2**, leaving the two sections in direct contradiction over whether Bruder's and Migne's editions are "critical"; **the new Possidius §2 bullet asserted Pontius's own *Life* "reaches this corpus only via" English translation**, false against row 194 (the Latin Vita), vendored in the very same pass and named three sections earlier in the same document; **§1's own corrected census parenthetical paired the `tradition`-only entry count (90) with the all-roles distinct-title count (97)**, a nine-too-high mismatch of scope; and **the M3 fix newly asserted row 191 was "the first Latin exception,"** when row 88 (the Codex Theodosianus) preceded it by three days — a fact Round 15's own M3 had already named. Round 16 also surfaced two further, genuine defects Round 15 had missed on its own first pass: **Registry row 196 and the CSEL 44 file's own header both wrongly claimed Letter 185 falls inside that file's own range** (it does not — Letter 185 opens CSEL 57 Pars IV, row 193, not CSEL 44), and **row 204's Boundary Status column read "Native" while its own Exclusion Reason column, on the same row, read "Excluded"** — a genuine internal contradiction Doc_02's own new disclosure then asserted the Excluded reading of as settled fact. Six further LOW and three COSMETIC findings (an unbalanced parenthesis, a broken "identifier below" pointer in two rows, an uncounted "roughly nine" that re-derivation puts at eleven, missing round-attribution on the eight corrected Registry rows, an overstated "not a subordinate's" characterization of Possidius against the vendored edition's own account of his and Augustine's relative ages, a quadrupled-asterisk markdown bug, and a silently-normalized OCR quotation) rounded out the count. All eighteen of Round 16's own findings (H1–H4, M1–M5, L1–L6, C1–C3) were fixed in the same pass this entry records, including writing this entry itself (M5's own second half) and adding the C2 disclosure Round 16 found missing (recorded above, in this entry's own Round 15 paragraph).

Independently re-checked against the file directly rather than trusted: the Content note's own claim is that the work's title appears "at the printed volume's p. 385," a different, later location than the half-title Round 19 read — and at that location (the file's own lines 45879–45888, immediately following the preface's own closing line and immediately preceding the chronicle's own first entry, "1 Adam cum e.sset annorum CCXXX") the file reads "PROSPERI TIRONIS / EPITOMA DE CHRONICON, / QITIBUS ET GENERATIONES..." — quoted here in the file's own literal OCR form, corrected here, independent review Round 20's own L4, from an earlier draft of this very paragraph that silently normalized "QITIBUS" to "QUIBUS" and "e.sset" to "esset," in a paragraph whose entire subject is whether a quotation matches its file — "DE" present, exactly as the file itself has it at that word. The volume genuinely carries two different title headings at two different locations, one without "DE" and one with it; the Content note quotes the second, correctly. Fixing this as Round 19 proposed would have replaced an accurate quotation with an inaccurate one — left as written, and logged here rather than silently declined, per this build's own standing rule that a finding is checked against the primary source before being acted on, not applied on the strength of the review asserting it.

**This fix pass closes Round 25 by direct instruction rather than by dispatching Round 26.** Every one of Round 25's fourteen findings is fixed above. Beyond the sites Round 25 itself named, this pass ran an explicit full-corpus sweep for each finding — grepping the complete document set (`Doc_02_Source_Ecology.md`, `Source_Registry.md`, `Source_Acquisition_Manifest.md`, this log, `cic/texts/REGISTRY.yaml`, `cic/texts/README.md`, every `cic/corpus-map/` atlas and staging file, and both `world-build-docs/_cross-world/` download-queue files) for every other instance of each finding's own underlying claim, phrase, or figure, not only the site each finding quoted — rather than patching each quoted line alone, the shape of error this log's own pattern paragraph has tracked since Round 16.
