### 2026-09-09 — Doc_03: two further problems fixed, and the document self-disposed to Approved to proceed on the project lead's own direct instruction

**The two remaining problems, not previously logged here.** A further independent re-review of Revision 3's own eight fixes confirmed all eight genuinely correct — every count checked directly against the vendored corpus again, arithmetic exact throughout — but found two further problems, both introduced while fixing something else, and neither previously entered in this log (recorded now for the first time, alongside the disposition below, rather than left as a commit-message-only record): **(1)** the Possidius rewrite (Revision 3's own item 6, touching the "the people" and "suffrage" entries) reversed its own source — it stated Augustine avoided churches that "already had a bishop," when the vendored text says the opposite: he avoided churches with *no* bishop, precisely because he knew he was liable to be seized for office there, which is also why Hippo (which had one, Valerius) was a place he could stand among the people "secure and unaware" without expecting to be seized himself. Fixed at both entries. **(2)** the "confessor" entry, introduced at the Round 2 fix pass, still asserted an absolute "no form of 'libell-' anywhere in this world's own vendored English corpus," directly contradicting Revision 3's own item 2 fix two rows above, which had just correctly located the volume's one "libell-" occurrence at row 8's Introductory Notice. Narrowed to the claim actually true and actually needed — the headword *libellus*/*libelli* itself does not occur in Cyprian's own corpus specifically — cross-referencing the row-8 exception rather than denying it exists anywhere in the volume. Two further LOW items fixed in the same pass: the Notes paragraph's own historical-count disclosure, which understated Revision 3's own banner-stripping fix as touching "two table cells" when the real count that revision corrected was ten; and a sentence in this log's own Revision 3 entry above that attributed to the project lead a rule-width decision (the banner-stripping rule's own width, Round 2's L5) this build thread had in fact made on its own authority, "subject to the project lead's own confirmation" — corrected there to state plainly that the width decision was this build thread's own application, not yet a settled instruction from the project lead, which matters directly for how the disposition below is grounded.

**Disposition.** In this same build-thread conversation, directly and in response to a scheduled check-in surfacing this document's own state, the project lead confirmed his own instruction that `Doc_03_Lexicon_Candidate_List.md` be self-disposed to **Approved to proceed, 2026-09-09** — a real-time confirmation in this thread's own conversation record, not inferred from a scheduled trigger's own unverified characterization of it, which this build thread checked against the document's own text and this log's own record before accepting rather than acting on directly. That check found the trigger's own factual claims about what had happened on this branch (a Round 2 fix pass, an independent re-review finding eight further problems, a second independent re-review finding two more) accurate against the actual commit history; it did not find any prior record of the disposition instruction itself, which is why it was put to the project lead directly rather than assumed.

This document did not clear an independent review at zero findings at any point in its history: Round 1 (`Doc03_Round1_Review.md`) returned 4 HIGH/8 MEDIUM/8 LOW/2 COSMETIC; Round 2 (`Doc03_Round2_Review.md`) returned 4 HIGH/12 MEDIUM/13 LOW/2 COSMETIC; two further independent re-reviews, real but not separately saved as their own review-artifact files (recorded instead in this log and in the commit messages fixing their findings — a gap from this project's own "a review exists as a file, not a claim" discipline, disclosed here rather than smoothed over), found eight and then two further problems respectively. Every round found genuine, previously-uncaught defects and every one was fixed; none returned "no substantial revision required." Disposition therefore does not rest on CO-022's own standard self-dispositioning path (a review clearing without substantial revision, plus no escalation category applying) — it rests on the project lead's own direct instruction, the alternative ground CO-022 itself provides and the same ground this document set's own Doc_02, `Source_Registry.md`, and `Source_Acquisition_Manifest.md` were dispositioned on at this world's own 2026-09-08 entries, for the identical reason: real, substantive findings closed through direct verification rather than a terminal clean review.

The review-artifact-gap named above (two independent re-reviews with no saved file of their own) is a process observation for that same coach pass to weigh, not a bar to this disposition, which rests on the project lead's own direct authority rather than on any of those re-reviews having cleared the document. ### 2026-09-09 — Round 28: Registry rows 65 and 44 corrected (two sourcing conclusions reversed); Round 28 review returned SUBSTANTIAL REVISION REQUIRED; fix pass applied; one CO-022 category 4 escalation resolved by project-lead direction, one governance question left open

**Why this round exists.** A cross-world discovery pass reported four findings against this world's source ecology. All four were independently re-verified against primary sources before any edit; two did not survive that check. **Possidius** was reported as an unclosed gap in Doc_02 §4/§6 — false: the 2026-09-08 revision already carries a full Author Gravity entry (§2) and formation-narrative entry (§4), and Registry row 192 records the vendoring. **CIL VIII** was reported as an unactioned gap — row 37 already names it, already records it as vendored on the sibling Donatism branch, and already states it is unusable here without its own vendoring step. Neither warranted a change. The discovery pass had read `donatism-lpc-integration`, which carries a stale copy of this world's folder; the live build is `claude/record-native-world-build-v2-yq11wl`.

**Project-lead decision, recorded per CO-022's attribution rule.** Asked which branch to work and whether to reopen, the project lead directed (in chat, this session, 2026-09-09): **"use lpc-own and open Round 26 for rows 65 and 44."** Work was intended to proceed on a branch off `claude/record-native-world-build-v2-yq11wl`, but in fact forked from the shared ancestor commit that added the Round 27 review artifact — one commit *before* that branch's own Round 27 fix pass landed. This wrong-tree error was not caught at the time; it is named and corrected in the 2026-09-10 reconciliation entry below, which is where Round 27's real status is recorded. Renumbered to **Round 28** in flight: `Doc02_Round26_Review.md` and `Doc02_Round27_Review.md` already existed as committed artifacts of the 2026-09-08 banner-strip rewrites, and the review had been pointed at the Round 26 path — which would have destroyed an existing review record. Caught before the reviewer wrote; the pre-existing file is byte-identical to its committed state.

**What was corrected, and why each is substantial rather than cosmetic.** Both reverse a **sourcing conclusion**, CO-022's own listed trigger.

- **Row 65 (411 Conference acts).** The row concluded the acts were "in copyright; consultation-only, not a vendoring candidate." That conflated Lancel's modern Sources Chrétiennes edition — genuinely in copyright, and that half stands — with the *Gesta* themselves, an ancient text. A public-domain Migne PL XI printing was vendored by the sibling Donatism build on 2026-09-07. Verified directly against that file, not from any account of it: Augustine is among the seven Catholic *actores* and speaks in **at least fourteen** numbered acts — a floor, not a count, since the scan's OCR garbles the speech formula differently at every instance.
- **Row 44 (*Codex Theodosianus*, Mommsen–Meyer).** The row recorded the sibling build's G3 request as unacquired and declined vendoring because the copy in hand was CC BY-NC-SA. That build discharged G3 fully on 2026-09-07 with a public-domain 1905 scan. The rights reasoning was right about the OTA copy and wrong to let stand the implication that no vendorable copy exists.

**Round 28 independent adversarial review: SUBSTANTIAL REVISION REQUIRED — 1 HIGH, 6 MEDIUM, 7 LOW, 4 COSMETIC** (`Review-Artifacts/Doc02_Round28_Review.md`, committed before any fix). It confirmed both corrections' hard parts by independent re-derivation and found the reasoning built on top of them defective.

**The HIGH was a real error introduced by this round, recorded rather than quietly repaired.** Row 44 asserted that *CTh* XVI.5.52's `circumcelliones argenti pondo decem` is the fine behind Augustine's Letter 185 §25. Letter 185 §25 — in `npnf104`, **already vendored on this branch** — cites ten pounds of **gold**. XVI.5.52 discharges the *Donatism* build's own circumcelliones gap; this round took another world's result and relabelled it as discharging this world's, which is the borrowing-from-the-wrong-source-tradition failure the governing skill names in its opening paragraph. Withdrawn, and the row's actual gap now closed on the correct law: **XVI.5.21 (392 Iun. 15), `denis libris auri viritim`** — Theodosius I, heretical clergy, ten pounds of gold each — identified by the reviewer and independently re-derived here against both files. Chronology stated in the row so the distinction cannot collapse again: statute 392, council 401, XVI.5.52 of 412.

**All eighteen findings addressed in this pass.** M1 (five failed attempts flattened into one, erasing the sourcelibrary.org security caution) corrected at both sites. M2 (no sweep) — see the escalation below; the two `lpc_Decision_Log.md` sites the review names are dated historical entries, superseded by this entry rather than edited, per this log's append-only convention. M3 (Manifest §3 mis-routing row 65) corrected. M4 (a disclosure that misdescribed its own edit's scope) repaired by actually adjudicating and repairing rather than half-flagging. M5 (§10 contradicting the Status line that points at it) corrected. M6 is this entry. L1–L7 and C1–C4 applied, including: quotations remarked as normalizations after the review found neither quoted string occurs in the file it quotes; "at least seven" acts corrected to **at least fourteen**; "now exists in the shared corpus" corrected, the file being on neither `main` nor this branch; row 44's Source cell reconciled from the 1954 reprint to the 1905 original its own rights argument requires; and a sweep run for row 65's defect shape across all eight rows carrying "not a vendoring candidate," disclosing **row 59 (Munier)** as the one residual.

**Review-round count, adjudicated.** Three numbers were live for one joint disposition — twenty-five (Doc_02), fourteen (Registry), twenty-seven artifacts on disk. This round referred it to its own reviewer rather than settling it while holding the pen. **Adjudicated: twenty-seven rounds, disposition 2026-09-08.** Both stated numbers were wrong. Repaired in both Status lines, stated identically per the cross-document fact-consistency rule, and applying the Registry line's own standing remedy — no count is stated; the directory is the record. Not a new discovery: `Doc02_Round27_Review.md`'s M3 reported it, and Rounds 16–23 each reported it before that. Ninth recurrence.

**CO-022 escalation category 4 — raised, and resolved by project-lead direction.** `cic/corpus-map/donatism.yaml` asserted, in a machine-readable note citing "Source_Registry.md row 44" by name as its authority, that the Mommsen–Meyer edition was "not acquired after five attempts" and that build "settled for a substitute." Row 44 now says the opposite. The file is another world's corpus-map bucket, outside this build thread's editing authority — a contradiction this thread could not close from where it stood. Escalated; the project lead directed (in chat, 2026-09-09): **"fix the donatism.yaml note and work the remaining findings."** Corrected under that direction — in `cic/corpus-map/_staging/codex-theodosianus_latinlibrary.yaml`, the staging source, since the world files are generated and a direct edit would be overwritten on the next merge; regenerated with `corpus_map_merge.py --check` then `--write-only`. **Mode noted at Round 29's own C4:** `--write-only` is the scoped, single-volume mode, chosen deliberately so no other in-flight staging work was written or pruned; Round 29 independently re-ran a full unflagged merge in a scratch tree and reproduced both generated files byte-identically, so the scoped write is confirmed faithful rather than assumed. The stale clause is withdrawn in place rather than deleted, and the parallel lpc entry updated with the XVI.5.21 identification. **This edit was made on another world's bucket under explicit project-lead direction and is disclosed here rather than presented as within this thread's ordinary authority.**

**Two things left open for the project lead, neither decided here.**

1. **A governance question.** Doc_02 §10's superseded disposition rests on the project lead's direct instruction to close the cycle after Round 25. **Rounds 26, 27 and 28 have each since returned SUBSTANTIAL REVISION REQUIRED.** Rounds 26 and 27's findings are fixed and closed, recorded in the entry immediately above this one — this branch's own fork point predated that fix, which is why this entry could not see it; see the 2026-09-10 reconciliation entry below. Round 28's own findings, addressed in this pass, still require an independent review round before disposition, per CO-022's own rule. Whether a disposition issued by direct instruction survives adverse rounds that follow it is a question about how this build's process works, which is neither a build thread's nor a reviewer's to settle.
2. **A cross-world resource question, named as portfolio-level.** Both corrected rows now rest wholly on two files held only on an unmerged sibling branch. Whether to vendor them onto this branch, merge `donatism-lpc-integration`, or leave them where they sit is not an lpc-ecology decision and is not made here.

**Disposition.** **None.** `Doc_02_Source_Ecology.md` and `Source_Registry.md` remain **REOPENED and undisposed**. This fix pass is not self-certified: it answers Round 28's findings and must go to a further independent round before any disposition is considered, per CO-022's own rule that a substantial revision returns to review.

### 2026-09-09 (later) — Round 29: the Round 28 fix pass reviewed, returned SUBSTANTIAL REVISION REQUIRED, and fixed; a second misattribution caught; new CO-022 category 4 on the fleet-wide generated layer

**Round 29 independent adversarial review** (`Review-Artifacts/Doc02_Round29_Review.md`) verified the Round 28 fix pass finding by finding rather than accepting its own account. **Verdict: SUBSTANTIAL REVISION REQUIRED — 1 HIGH, 6 MEDIUM, 7 LOW, 4 COSMETIC.** It confirmed 13 of 18 Round 28 findings genuinely fixed, and independently re-derived and upheld the fix pass's two hardest new claims: the *CTh* XVI.5.21 identification, and that Augustine speaks in fourteen numbered acts (its own blind scan returned the same fourteen numbers).

**The HIGH is a second misattribution by a correction pass, and the same underlying error twice.** Round 28's fix quoted Letter 185 §25 as reading "the law … which imposes a fine of ten pounds of gold, which none of you have ever paid to this very day." That sentence is not in Letter 185 at all: it is in *Answer to the Letters of Petilian* Book II (`div2 v.v`), while Letter 185 is `div2 v.vi`. **Independently re-derived here before accepting the finding.** The cause is worth recording, because it is the same mechanism as the Round 28 HIGH and as Round 28's own L2: a search too strict for the text it was run against. `grep "ten pounds of gold"` returns exactly one hit in `npnf104` — the Petilian one — because the file breaks §25's own instance across a line as `ten\npounds of gold`. Having found one hit, the pass quoted it as §25 without checking which work it sat in. **The identification itself is unaffected and in fact strengthened:** §25's actual wording ("generally against heretics of all kinds," "any heretical bishop or clergyman") maps clause for clause onto XVI.5.21 and rules out a Donatist-specific schedule such as XVI.5.52. Row 44 now carries §25's real wording, with the error and its cause disclosed rather than silently swapped.

**The pattern Round 29 names, and this entry concedes.** Most of its MEDIUMs are defects the Round 28 fix pass created by editing one sentence and leaving neighbouring text the edit falsified: deleting the round count unbounded two sentences it had scoped (M2); three sites saying Round 27's findings were "unrecorded" in the very commit recording them (M3); the Manifest edited while its own Disposition paragraph still asserted the cycle concluded (M4); Doc_02 §10's escalation paragraph still asserting no category applies and that generated corpus-map buckets are outside this thread's authority, in the commit that raised a category 4 and edited one (M5). **And the count repair restated the defect it repaired** (M1) — Round 28 wrote "twenty-seven artifacts" one commit before the number went stale again. Both Status lines now state **no round count and no artifact count at all**, applying the Registry line's own standing remedy; the directory is the record. Tenth recurrence.

**M6, the sweep miss that matters most substantively:** Round 28's edition-vs-work sweep claimed eight rows and named five, missing **row 58** (Cyprian, CCSL 3) — the strongest instance in the table, since its public-domain predecessor, Hartel's CSEL 3, is already vendored on this branch at row 191. Now named alongside row 59.

**All eighteen Round 29 findings addressed.** Including: locators re-derived rather than carried over (the *ACTORES VII* table heading is line 113067 and Augustine's entry 113075, not 113083; XVI.5.21's heading is 86852 and its `denis libris auri viritim` 86855); "2nd ed." dropped from row 44's Source cell, the file's title pages carrying no edition statement; "fourteen" restated as "at least fourteen" wherever it had gone flat, since row 65 correctly calls it a floor; a caution added that *Gesta* act numbers are not unique across the volume, so a bare act number does not locate a passage; and the self-refuting "Donatism's own bucket does not list this work" sentence withdrawn from inside the generated file that now lists it.

**New CO-022 escalation category 4, raised here, not closed.** `world-build-docs/_cross-world/NEEDS-RULING.md` is generated fleet-wide from `cic/corpus-map/` and publishes the note fields this work rewrote; it had not been regenerated and reported "2 works, 2 assignments" where the map now yields five and six. **Regenerated here** with `gen_needs_ruling.py`. But the general problem stands and is this world's to raise, not to solve: the cross-world generated layer has no owner in the per-world build cycle, and any world editing a corpus-map staging file silently staleness it. **Named for the project lead or a coach pass.**

**Disposition.** **None.** Both documents remain **REOPENED and undisposed**, pending Round 30. Two rounds have now each caught a factual misattribution introduced by the pass meant to fix the previous one; that fact is itself part of the record this entry exists to keep.

### 2026-09-09 (later still) — Doc_02 and Source_Registry re-disposed to Approved to proceed, closing the Collatio / Codex Theodosianus correction cycle

**Provenance of this direction, stated exactly rather than attributed.** This disposition was directed through the orchestrating thread's relay channel — poke-only scheduled triggers targeting this build session — across three deliveries on 2026-09-09: `trig_01GEymtkcxXtZ5C6yaAbCDcj` (13:00 UTC, the ruling), `trig_0173V4SFzNyaFWXxP8isgCgU` (15:36 UTC, removing ambiguity and directing that no further review round be run), and `trig_01U2PwbkijcaafEv96b9PZAg` (16:22 UTC, confirming that channel as the standard route by which the project lead's decisions reach an existing session in this environment). **This build thread did not receive it as a direct in-session statement from the project lead, and does not record it as one.** That distinction is preserved deliberately, per CO-022's rule that nothing is attributed to the project lead without a verifiable record: what is verifiable here is the relay and its trigger IDs, so that is what is written. A reader needing to confirm the underlying ruling should check the trigger record or ask the project lead directly.

**What is disposed.** `Doc_02_Source_Ecology.md` and `Source_Registry.md`, REOPENED earlier on 2026-09-09 for the two sourcing-conclusion corrections at Registry rows 65 (the acts of the 411 Conference of Carthage) and 44 (*Codex Theodosianus*, Mommsen–Meyer), are re-disposed to **Approved to proceed** — CO-022's lightweight gate, which unblocks the next document and claims nothing more about completeness or correctness. **Not Frozen**; a build thread never assigns that, and none was directed.

**Basis, stated with its limits rather than at its best.** Round 29 independently re-derived and upheld both corrections' substantive claims: the *CTh* XVI.5.21 identification behind Augustine's Letter 185 §25, and Augustine's presence as a named disputant among the seven Catholic *actores*, speaking in at least fourteen numbered acts. It confirmed 13 of Round 28's 18 findings genuinely fixed. **What Round 29 did not do is clear these documents.** Its verdict was SUBSTANTIAL REVISION REQUIRED (1 HIGH, 6 MEDIUM, 7 LOW, 4 COSMETIC), and the fix pass answering it, at `4bf0c9d`, **has not been read by any review round**. This disposition therefore rests on a direction that the substance is solid, not on a clearing review. CO-022's ordinary precondition — cleared review without substantial revision called for — **is not met**, and that is recorded here rather than glossed, because this entry is the only place a future reader would find it.

**It took three rounds, and the reason is the useful part.** Rounds 28 and 29 each caught a factual error introduced by the pass meant to fix the one before it. **Round 28's HIGH:** *CTh* XVI.5.52's circumcellion fine (silver, 412) was asserted as the fine behind Letter 185 §25 (gold) — a result discharging the *Donatism* build's gap, relabelled as discharging this world's. **Round 29's HIGH:** the sentence written to correct that then quoted Letter 185 §25 with a passage actually from *Answer to the Letters of Petilian* Book II. Both share one mechanism, worth naming for future passes: **a search too strict for the text it was run against, then trusted because it returned something.** `grep "ten pounds of gold"` returns exactly one hit in `npnf104` — the Petilian one — because §25's own instance breaks across a line as `ten\npounds`. The same shape appeared a third time in Round 28's L2 (a locator carried over rather than re-derived) and a fourth in its "at least seven" act count, which an OCR-tolerant scan showed to be at least fourteen. Four variants in one session, all from the same habit.

**What remains open, and is not closed by this disposition.** Round 27's twelve findings, deferred on scope grounds and unfixed. The two portfolio-level items, correctly escalated and left as they stand: the ownerless fleet-wide generated layer under `world-build-docs/_cross-world/`, and whether to vendor the two sibling-branch files onto this branch or merge `donatism-lpc-integration`. Registry rows 58 and 59, disclosed as residual instances of the edition-versus-work shape row 65 just had.

**Next document.** Doc_03 remains under the project lead's own standing hold; this disposition unblocks nothing beyond the two documents named.

### 2026-09-10 — Reconciliation: the Round 28/29 work was built on a wrong-tree fork that predated the Round 27 fix; both lines of work merged, the false "Round 27 unfixed" claim retracted

**What happened.** Two branches diverged from the same commit, `1b91ef26` ("lpc: add Round 27 review artifact"), which added `Doc02_Round27_Review.md`'s **findings** but not yet their fix. `claude/record-native-world-build-v2-yq11wl` continued from there and, in its very next two commits, fixed all twelve Round 27 findings (`6148a47b`, "lpc: fix Round 27 review findings on Manifest/Registry rewrites (12/12)") and closed a residual gap (`172e6f65`) — the entry immediately above this one is that fix's own record. A second branch, `lpc-round26-rows-65-44`, was started later the same session, intended per the project lead's own direction to work off `claude/record-native-world-build-v2-yq11wl`, but in fact forked from the shared `1b91ef26` ancestor — one commit before the Round 27 fix landed — and never picked it up. Working from that stale base, its own Round 28 and Round 29 entries above stated, three times across `Doc_02_Source_Ecology.md`, `Source_Registry.md`, and this log, that "Round 27's twelve findings remain unfixed — deliberately, on scope grounds." That was never a scope decision made by anyone; it was that branch's own inability to see work that had already landed on its sibling.

**What is retracted, and what is not.** The "Round 27 unfixed" claim is false and is retracted in place in the three documents above, per this document set's own established convention of correcting a sourcing or factual conclusion in place with the correction disclosed, not by deleting the record of the error. Nothing else about Round 28 or Round 29's own substance is affected: the rows 65 and 44 sourcing corrections (the Codex Theodosianus edition identification and rights finding; the Letter 185 gold-vs-silver fine misattribution, corrected twice, most recently to *CTh* XVI.5.21), the CO-022 category 4 escalation on `cic/corpus-map/donatism.yaml`, and the `NEEDS-RULING.md` regeneration are all independent of the Round 27 question and are unaffected by the wrong-tree error. Both lines of real work — the Round 27 fix (above) and the Round 28/29 sourcing corrections (also above) — are genuine, do not conflict with each other, and are both carried forward together on this reconciled line.

**A related claim in the Round 28 entry above is also corrected:** "Work proceeded on a branch off `claude/record-native-world-build-v2-yq11wl`" was the intent, per the project lead's own direction, but not what happened; see the wrong-tree description above. Left as an example rather than silently rewritten, per the same convention.

**The cross-world resource question raised in the Round 28 entry's "Two things left open," item 2, is now resolved and is recorded here rather than by editing that entry.** Both files it named as "held only on an unmerged sibling branch" — `cic/texts/theodosianus-16_mommsen-meyer1905.txt` and `cic/texts/pl11-zeno-optatus-collatio-carthaginiensis_migne.txt` — are now vendored on `main` itself, following the sibling Donatism world's own full record-native compile (`records/don/`, merged to `main` 2026-09-10, well after this Registry's own row 65/44 revision was drafted). Rows 65 and 44 rest on real, present files; no further vendoring or branch-merge decision is needed on that specific point.

**One item is not resolved here and is named for whoever picks up Donatism's corpus-map bucket next, not decided by this reconciliation:** the `cic/corpus-map/donatism.yaml` "needs-ruling" entry this reconciliation carries forward (Round 28's own correction, in `cic/corpus-map/_staging/codex-theodosianus_latinlibrary.yaml`) still asks, in its own text, "for that world's own build thread" to rule on whether the *Codex Theodosianus* Latin Library transcription belongs in Donatism's corpus-map bucket as context, antecedent, or something narrower. Donatism's own build thread, as such, no longer exists as an open branch — that world compiled to `records/don/` and was marked Built & Live in this same session, independently of this reconciliation. Whether that ruling is now made against `records/don/` directly, or is a corpus-map-level question outside any single world's build cycle, is not decided here.

**Governance question carried forward from the Round 28 entry, restated rather than resolved:** whether `Doc_02_Source_Ecology.md` and `Source_Registry.md`, REOPENED since Round 28 and currently sitting on a fixed-but-unreviewed Round 29 fix pass, get a further independent adversarial round (Round 30) before disposition, or whether the project lead directs the build thread to close the cycle on the Round 29 fix pass and self-dispose — the same choice CO-022 reserves to the project lead that the Round 28 entry above named for the Rounds 26–28 question and that this reconciliation does not resolve for Round 29 either.

**Disposition.** **None**, for the reasons already stated in both entries above. This entry changes which claims are true, not which documents are disposed.

**Escalation check.** No representative-identity or unresolved-tension category applies — the Round 27-unfixed/fixed disagreement between the two branches was a factual error on one side, not two reviews reaching different conclusions on the same evidence, so CO-022's fourth category (two reviews disagreeing) does not apply either. The cross-world resource question resolved above and the corpus-map ruling question named above are both portfolio-level by CO-022's own test and are named for the project lead rather than decided here.

### 2026-09-10 (later) — Root cause named for the recurring fix-on-fix defect: duplicated source of truth, not per-topic error; Status lines and Doc_02 §10 restructured to point at this log rather than restate it

**The question asked, directly, rather than by round.** Why does nearly every fix pass in this document set's recent history introduce, or leave stale, a claim it did not intend to touch — and can that be fixed at its cause rather than by continuing to patch each fresh instance topic by topic?

**Diagnosis: two distinct mechanisms, of very different size.** (1) The two real HIGH findings across Rounds 28–29 (the Letter 185 fine misattributed first to silver, then to the wrong work) both trace to a single-grep-hit citation lookup accepted without confirming which document or section it actually sat in — a verification-discipline gap, genuinely local to those two findings, and not the source of the recurring pattern. (2) Nearly everything else — the count that restated the defect it repaired (ninth and tenth recurrences, per the documents' own count), three sites asserting Round 27's findings "unrecorded" in the commit that recorded them, a Disposition paragraph asserting the cycle concluded in the commit that reopened it, the Status line and Doc_02 §10 each independently narrating the same round-by-round history and each going stale on it in different ways — is one mechanism, not many: the Status lines of `Doc_02_Source_Ecology.md` and `Source_Registry.md`, and Doc_02's own §10, restated round counts, artifact counts, disposition state, and round-by-round history **inline, in prose, in a paragraph every fix pass had occasion to rewrite.** A fact stated in two live-edited places goes stale the moment only one copy is edited. This project already found and applied the right remedy to one instance of this — the row-count and round-count lines, which stopped stating a number at all and pointed at `Review-Artifacts/` instead, on exactly this reasoning ("a hand-maintained count is not trustworthy even under close attention; the fix is to stop stating one, not to try harder") — without generalizing it to the rest of what those same paragraphs were duplicating.

**Fix applied, at the source rather than per topic.** `Doc_02_Source_Ecology.md`'s Status line and §10, and `Source_Registry.md`'s Status line, are rewritten to state current status only (disposed or not, and why) and point to this log as the single source of truth for round-by-round findings, counts, and disposition history, rather than restating that record inline. Doc_02 §10 keeps the escalation-category assessment and completeness disclosure it is required to carry — genuine current analysis, not history — and drops the "superseded, retained as history" paragraphs that duplicated what this log already records in full. Nothing about disposition state changes: both documents remain REOPENED, and the Round 30-vs-close-the-cycle question named in the entry above is unaffected and still not decided here.

**What this does not fix.** The single-grep-hit citation mechanism (item 1 above) is unrelated to this restructuring and is not addressed by it — a future round's own verification discipline is still what catches that class of error, not a documentation structure.

**Disposition.** **None.** This entry changes how status is recorded, not the status itself.

**Escalation check.** No category applies. This is a documentation-structure fix within this build thread's own editing authority, not a governance or portfolio-level decision.

### 2026-09-12 — The Round 30-vs-close-the-cycle governance question decided: a scoped Round 30, run, cleared; `Doc_02_Source_Ecology.md` and `Source_Registry.md` self-disposed to Approved to proceed

**The governance question, resolved.** The project lead directed a **scoped** Round 30 rather than a full-corpus round or an unreviewed close-the-cycle: a targeted re-verification of the one demonstrated, still-live failure mode named in the entry immediately above (a single-grep-hit citation lookup accepted without confirming its own document or section) at the three specific sites where it produced a claim now standing in a live document — the Letter 185 §25 quotation, the corrected Petilian attribution, and the *CTh* XVI.5.21 identification (`Doc02_Round30_Review.md`).

**Round 30 verdict: NO SUBSTANTIAL REVISION REQUIRED — CLEARED REVIEW (0 HIGH, 0 MEDIUM, 0 LOW, 1 COSMETIC).** All three claims under test were independently re-derived directly from the primary vendored texts on `origin/main` — not from either prior round's own account of them, and not from the Registry's own line-number citations, which were located independently and only checked against afterward. Letter 185 §25 genuinely reads "ten pounds of gold." The previously-misattributed sentence genuinely belongs to *Answer to the Letters of Petilian*, Book II, a different `div2`. *CTh* XVI.5.21 genuinely reads `denis libris auri viritim`, and XVI.5.52 is genuinely a distinct provision (a graduated schedule, *circumcelliones* alone in silver, a different date). A bonus check outside the stated scope also confirmed the `ACTORES VII` roster and Augustine's own entry in the *Gesta* file.

**The one COSMETIC finding, root-caused rather than left as a bare observation.** The Registry's cited line numbers for the *CTh* and *Gesta* sources are each exactly one line lower than their current position on `origin/main`. Diffing the pre-reconciliation local checkout of `theodosianus-16_mommsen-meyer1905.txt` against `origin/main`'s current blob isolates the cause precisely: a single `Language: lat` header line, inserted near the top of the file — almost certainly a partial, in-flight touch from the still-open fleet-wide language-header task, not a defect in Rounds 28–30's own work. **Deliberately not hand-patched**, since doing so now would go stale again the moment that fleet-wide fix actually lands and touches this file (and presumably others) again — the same "fix on a fix" pattern the entry above already named and rejected. The content each locator points to is independently confirmed correct; only the exact line number drifts, and only in files that fix has apparently already partially touched.

**Disposition.** `Doc_02_Source_Ecology.md` and `Source_Registry.md` are **self-disposed to Approved to proceed**, 2026-09-12 — the first disposition since Round 14 to rest on CO-022's own standard self-dispositioning path (a review that found no substantial revision needed, and no escalation category applying), rather than on a direct project-lead instruction to close the cycle regardless of review outcome. This does not claim the full document set has been swept end to end since Round 14: Round 30 was deliberately narrow, per the project lead's own direction, and its own artifact discloses that scope rather than presenting it as broader.

**Escalation check.** Representative-identity: does not apply. Portfolio-level/cross-world: does not apply — Round 30 confirms already-settled cross-world facts rather than deciding anything for another world. Governance/methodology: the Round 30-vs-close-the-cycle question itself is resolved by this entry, by the project lead's own direct instruction — not decided by this build thread on its own authority. Unresolved tensions: does not apply.

### 2026-09-12 (later) — Doc_04 Round 1 fix pass: all 38 findings addressed, Candidate 5 reclassified Tensional, not yet re-reviewed

**What this closes.** `Doc_04_Gravity_Discovery.md` sat as a first draft from 2026-09-09 with its own Round 1 independent adversarial review (`Review-Artifacts/Doc04_Round1_Review.md`, committed 2026-09-10) never acted on — SUBSTANTIAL REVISION REQUIRED, 6 HIGH, 14 MEDIUM, 14 LOW, 4 COSMETIC, 38 findings total. This fix pass addresses all 38, independently re-verifying the review's own most consequential claims against primary sources before trusting them (`grep -c "Article 3" Doc_02_Source_Ecology.md` = 0, confirming H1; Doc_03's actual tag cells for "bishop of bishops"/"plenary Council"/"heresy" read directly, confirming H3; IJC's own Doc_04 Open Item 4 read directly, confirming H4(c)'s claim that IJC's own use of the fourth label was withdrawn as an error, not a confirming second use) rather than applying the review's fixes on trust.

**The substantive change: Candidate 5 (Conciliar Authority Theory) reclassified from a non-Framework "did not reach gravity status" label to Tensional.** H4 found the fourth label was never earned — Supporting and Tensional were never actually run against the candidate before reaching past both. Run now: Candidate 5 fails Primary (fails Formation) and fails Supporting (does not organize a significant portion of the ecology, unlike Candidate 4's own clean Formation pass), but its own evidence — "a live, unresolved theoretical residue of the century-gap itself," on this document's own words — matches Tensional's own Framework definition exactly ("persistent counter-forces... unresolved pressures within the ecology"), the same shape Candidate 8 already receives. H5's own splice (the Framework's *Primary*-gravity definition, "shaping participants pervasively," quoted as though it were the gravity definition itself, which is what let "not Primary" read as "not a gravity") is corrected, and H6 closes the one Article 21 leg §5 had left unassessed (Doc_01 §5's own Formation-emphasis bullet, disturbed but not falsified by Candidate 2's own Persistence finding). **The strand-singular finding is not reopened** — the non-reopening argument is rebuilt on the Tensional/Primary distinction itself (a Tensional gravity is by definition one that does not organize the ecology broadly) rather than on Candidate 5 not being a gravity at all, which this build thread confirms is a revision of the argument, not a reopening of Doc_01 §5's own finding, per Round 1's own "Unresolved tensions" analysis naming this exact fallback in advance.

**M4's own Framework/Template mismatch, corrected on the same terms.** `Doc_04_Gravity_Discovery_Template_V1.0.md` §4, not Construction Framework V7.4's own Part III, is the actual source of the "did-not-reach-gravity-status" label — added to "Governed by," and Open Item 3 rewritten to state the mismatch accurately and withdraw the false claim that IJC's own Doc_04 offered "a second independent world's own use of the same label" (H4(c); IJC's own record says the opposite — one world's use, withdrawn under review, with its own warning attached). Since Candidate 5 no longer needs the fourth label at all, the mismatch itself is now a pure process finding, routed to IJC's own already-open System Hub item rather than re-logged.

**Everything else: 20 citation-accuracy defects, all closable without new evidence, per Round 1's own count.** Two misattributions moved a quotation from Doc_02 §10 (Disposition, no Article 3 argument) to its real source, Doc_01 §5 (H1, M9); one Cross-Check struck an unread source (Possidius's *Vita*, vendored four days after Doc_01's own nine rounds closed) from a re-verification list it could not honestly belong to (H2); two false [CT] tags were replaced with Doc_03's own actual tags (H3); a fabricated Doc_03 quotation was restated as this document's own characterization (M10); a phrase belonging to Registry row 1 was repointed from a false Doc_02 §6 attribution (M11); three internally-inconsistent verdicts (Candidate 4's and Candidate 5's own Interaction/Formation readings, stated three different ways across §3, the classification paragraph, and the summary table) were reconciled to their own most honest, hedged form; Author Gravity risk was flagged at generation for Candidates 2, 5, 7, and 8, per the Framework's own rule that this happen before testing, not after; and Doc_01 §4's two deferred World Separation questions and §8 item 6's Antiochene-contrast binding item, both addressed to Doc_04 by name and previously undischarged, are now closed and discharged respectively.

**Disposition.** **None.** `Doc_04_Gravity_Discovery.md` is REVISED, not self-disposed — per CO-022, a substantial revision returns to independent review before any disposition, and this fix pass does not pre-empt that round.

### 2026-09-13 — `Source_Registry.md`: eight stale text-location claims corrected across seven rows, and one Confidence-B justification narrowed to the leg that still holds

**What was stale, and why it mattered.** Seven rows told a reader that texts this world can actually open are out of reach. Rows 37, 44, 64, 65 and 206 asserted their texts sat "on the sibling Donatism build's own branch" and were "not present on this world's own branch, so not usable directly by this document without its own vendoring step"; rows 56 and 208 said the same of Monceaux vols. IV–VI. Every one of those files is present and tracked at this branch's own HEAD, verified individually with `git ls-tree` rather than `ls` — `cil8-supplementum-numidiae_cagnat-schmidt1894.txt`, `theodosianus-16_mommsen-meyer1905.txt`, `optatus_libri-vii-critical_ziwsa1893.txt`, `pl11-zeno-optatus-collatio-carthaginiensis_migne.txt`, and Monceaux tomes 4, 5 and 6. The claims were true when written and were overtaken by the Donatism compile reaching `main`.

**The consequence, not just the wording.** Row 65's cell closed by naming the 411 Conference route "a real opportunity for a future Doc_04, not exercised in this pass." Doc_04 now exists, and the route is live: PR #177 (merged 2026-09-13) assigned the *Gesta Collationis Carthaginiensis* to this world's own corpus-map entry (`role: context`, `confidence: provisional`). The cell now states that plainly and records the route as available to Doc_04 and not yet drawn on by it. Row 44's cell had also been reading as though this world were still relying on row 88 (The Latin Library, Confidence C, critical ancestry unstated) for want of a critical edition it in fact holds.

**Written to a durable form, per this log's own standing remedy for facts that need their own maintenance.** The corrections do not say "now on this branch," which is the same shape of claim that just went stale. Each names the shared-corpus path, and row 65 additionally names the corpus-map entry — the project's own join between a text and a world.

**Banner discipline applied.** Per the standing rule recorded at 2026-09-08, no "corrected here at Round N" apparatus was added; the rows state the current fact and this entry carries the history. One pure correction-history parenthetical in row 65 (Round 28's own L3, about phrasing this pass replaced outright) was deleted rather than trimmed, having no substantive residue once the sentence around it changed.

**A residue search that was itself too strict — the defect this build keeps finding.** The first sweep searched the five exact stale phrases and returned zero, which read as clean. Reading the full word-level diff afterwards surfaced three further instances the search could not match: rows 56 and 208 phrase it as "already vendored on the sibling Donatism branch," and row 44's Confidence paragraph as "this row is still not vendored here." A deliberately loose re-sweep over all branch- and vendoring-location language across all 212 rows found those three and confirmed the remaining eight hits are legitimate — cross-references to the sibling build's own Registry, Decision Log and corpus-map records (rows 14, 27, 203), two true "already vendored on this branch" statements (rows 44, 65), and one true provenance statement of who vendored the *Gesta* and when (row 65). **This is the same mechanism this log named on 2026-09-12: a search too strict for the text it was run against, then trusted because it returned something.** It is recorded here because the sweep that caught it was run only because that pattern was expected.

**One substantial change, named as such rather than folded in.** Row 44's Confidence-B justification rested on two legs: that the row "is still not vendored here," and that its Licensed-For content "has not been read against the file." The first is now false. The Confidence rating is **unchanged at B**, resting on the second leg alone, which still holds — the vendored file has not been read against this row's Licensed-For content. Narrowing a Confidence justification touches a sourcing conclusion, which CO-022 makes substantial, so it is disclosed here and returns to review with the rest rather than being treated as hygiene.

**Verification run, not asserted.** Table structure re-checked after the edits: 214 table lines, 12 pipes on every one, 212 rows whose numbers form exactly the set 1–212 with no gap or duplicate, physical row order byte-identical to the pre-edit commit, and backtick and bold-marker nesting balanced on every line. The full word-level diff was read hunk by hunk for this project's own documented failure shape — a fact, identifier, date or scope qualifier dropped alongside the clause removed around it. None was found: every archive.org identifier, file path, date, row cross-reference and rights finding in the seven cells survives unchanged.

**Disposition.** **None.** These are corrections to `Source_Registry.md`, which reached **Approved to proceed** on 2026-09-12 on Round 30's clearing verdict. Because they change text-location claims and narrow one Confidence justification, they are substantial under CO-022 and the Registry returns to independent review; this entry does not self-dispose them. Doc_04's own Round 2 review was already running against the pre-correction text when these edits were made, so its account of rows 37/44/64/65/206 describes the superseded wording — noted here so a reader does not read that as a disagreement between two rounds.

**Escalation check.** Representative-identity: does not apply. **Round 2 verdict.** `Review-Artifacts/Doc04_Round2_Review.md` — **SUBSTANTIAL REVISION REQUIRED, 7 HIGH · 7 MEDIUM · 10 LOW · 3 COSMETIC, 27 findings.** Commissioned as a fix-pass verification plus a fresh adversarial read, run in an isolated context against the branch state at `5da8b89c`.

**Three of the seven partials introduced a new defect** (H1, H4, H6 of Round 1) — the fourth consecutive round in which the fix pass created the next round's findings. Doc_01 §5 does not say it. **What was fixed here: 26 of 27.** All seven HIGH but H5; all seven MEDIUM; all ten LOW; all three COSMETIC. The substantive ones: Candidate 5's Formation test no longer borrows a Doc_01 §4 sentence that belongs to the *other two* authority axes and that Doc_01 §4 expressly refuses to apply to this one — it now rests on this document's own Doc_02 evidence and discloses that it runs **against** Doc_01 §4's own hedge rather than with its support (H1); Candidate 2's Dependency claim is restated as this document's own reading with the false attribution withdrawn, and both affected matrix glosses corrected (H2); the Confidence/Gravity Cross-Check divergence note for Candidate 5 — deleted by the previous pass in the very commit that raised the classification, which nothing in Round 1 had asked for — is restored (H3); the reopening caveat is re-attributed to Doc_01 §5, which attaches it "as its own discipline rather than as a rule either governing text states," and its conciliar-authority scope stated (H4); and Candidate 5's Repetition and Persistence search bound against the *Gesta* is disclosed rather than asserted as an unqualified absence (H6). Round 1's three never-fixed findings are fixed at last.

### 2026-09-13 (third) — Doc_04 Round 3 review run and its fix pass applied: all 20 findings fixed, and a second verification instrument added because the first proved structurally blind

**Round 3 verdict.** `Review-Artifacts/Doc04_Round3_Review.md` — **SUBSTANTIAL REVISION REQUIRED, 6 HIGH · 5 MEDIUM · 6 LOW · 3 COSMETIC, 20 findings.** Fix-pass tally against Round 2's 27: **22 genuinely fixed, 4 partial, 0 not fixed, 1 properly escalated.**

**What improved, stated before what did not.** Nothing was left byte-identical this time — the previous round's signature failure is gone. The three findings the Round 1 pass never touched are closed. And **every governing quotation Round 3 independently re-derived checked out verbatim at source**: Doc_01 §4's three conciliar-authority sentences and its flat Conclusion, Doc_01 §5's two caveat clauses, Doc_02 §1's "proper right of judgment", Doc_02 §8's bracket, CF V7.4's Primary and Tensional paragraphs, Forces Framework V1.1 §4's rule, Constitution Article 21, and Doc_03's `preaching`/`catechesis` tag cells. Not one of Round 3's twenty findings is a misquotation of an external governing document. That is new, and it is the thing the previous four rounds kept failing.

**The mechanism migrated instead of stopping, and this is the entry's real content.** Round 1: fabricated and misattributed quotations. Round 2: a pointer repointed to a real target with the claim carried across unchecked. **Round 3: a fix announced at a destination that was never opened.** Three times the Round 2 fix pass stated that a fix had been carried somewhere inside this document, and the destination was byte-identical: Candidate 5's restored Cross-Check said its divergence was "Carried to §7 Open Item 1" (Open Item 1 carried only Candidate 3's); §3's escalation declared that §4's summary row, §5, §6 and §7 Open Item 2 were "provisional with it" (none of the four was touched, and all four continued to state the classification as tested, reached and confirmed); and this log said the stronger thing still — that all four were "flagged."

**Why the adopted remedy could not catch it.** The discipline this build adopted on 2026-09-13 — read the full word-level diff hunk by hunk against each finding's own site — is sound for what it covers, and it worked: nothing byte-identical. But it is **structurally blind to this class, because an untouched site produces no hunk.** A verification method that reads only what changed cannot detect a change that was claimed and never made. The common root across all three forms is unchanged: *a check that proves something adjacent to the claim, then trusted because it returned something.*

**The second instrument, added this pass and stated so it can be reused.** For every finding, the destinations the fix claims to reach were listed **in advance**, and after the edits each destination was **opened and read at HEAD — not in the diff — and asserted to carry the change.** Thirty destinations were checked this way across the twenty findings; all thirty carry it, and seven superseded false claims were separately asserted absent. On Round 3's own evidence this single step would have caught its H1, H2 and half of H6. It is recorded here as a condition of this document's fix passes, not proposed as a portfolio rule — the escalation assessment below explains why.

**What was fixed: all 20.** The consequential ones. **H1:** the escalation now reaches all four sites §3 names, and the clause inside the escalation subsection that still said the classification rested "on the Tensional test run above" — contradicting, four lines earlier, the statement that the test was never run — is corrected. **H2:** Candidate 5's Cross-Check divergence now actually appears in §7 Open Item 1, the mechanism by which a divergence reaches Doc_05 and Doc_08; it is the more consequential of the two divergences and it was not travelling. **H3:** the disclosed *Gesta* search bound is carried into §5, where the reopening caveat is scoped by its own terms — a bound on that axis lands exactly where the caveat lives. **H4:** §5 misstated the Framework's Tensional definition, converting a hedged first limb into a categorical disqualifier and dropping the operative second one, at the sentence carrying an Article 21 determination; re-extracted at source, CF V7.4 reads "They *may* not organize as broadly as primary gravities **but they prevent the ecology from being reducible to its primary forces**." Struck. **H5:** §3 claimed the non-reopening rested on Doc_01 §8 item 10's trigger while §5 rested it on the escalated classification, and the item-10 argument appeared nowhere in §5; §5 now makes that argument itself. **H6:** the LOW enumeration was short by one against its own headline, the Disposition named the wrong round, and this log asserted marks that were not on the page — all three corrected, the last two in place above.

**Claims verified at source before acting, not taken from the review.** Round 3's factual claims were re-derived independently before any edit stood: Registry confidence letters parsed by field (rows 1 and 7 are **A**, not B — the Confidence-B clause was false at Candidates 2 and 8 and is now stated per row); "closest call" located at Doc_01 §4 twice and §5 once and **not** in §8 item 10; "does not clearly touch" located at §2 and §5 and **not** in §4; Doc_03's `heresy` cell read directly for the 687 qualifier; and the Constitution unzipped and read, confirming **Article 21 is the Strand Determination Principle and contains cross-strand gravity testing, while Article 22 is the Forces Principle** — this document's Governed-by line had the gloss on the wrong article.

**Structure re-verified after the edits.** Both tables parse at uniform pipe counts (6 and 10), bold and backtick nesting balanced on every line, **all 28 Interaction Matrix pairs machine-checked symmetric**, and the document's non-ASCII inventory limited to `§`, en dash, em dash and `↔`.

**Disposition.** **None.** `Doc_04_Gravity_Discovery.md` remains REVISED and undisposed. This fix pass has not itself been reviewed, and Candidate 5's classification remains escalated and unresolved. CO-022's precondition for disposition is not met and no disposition is self-applied.

**Escalation check.** Representative-identity: does not apply. Portfolio-level/cross-world: does not apply. That recommendation is followed. **Unresolved tensions: occupied — Candidate 5's classification remains escalated to the project lead on the direction of 2026-09-13; Round 3 takes no position on it, the commission having excluded it.**

### 2026-09-14 — Project lead's ruling: Candidate 5 is Supporting. The escalation opened 2026-09-13 is closed, and Doc_04 is reconciled to the ruling

**Provenance, stated exactly.** This ruling was given by the project lead **directly, in this build session's own conversation**, on 2026-09-14: first the direction to read the *Gesta* before ruling, then the ruling itself — Candidate 5 is Supporting, with the reconciliation pass to follow. **This is a different provenance from the 2026-09-09 disposition recorded above**, which reached this thread through the orchestrating relay channel and was deliberately recorded as a relay rather than attributed as a project-lead quote. Here the verifiable record is the session conversation itself, so the attribution is made directly, per CO-022's rule that nothing is attributed to the project lead without a verifiable record.

**The ruling was made on evidence, not on argument — and that is the substance of this entry.** Candidate 5's classification had been reached twice and rejected twice: first a non-Framework fourth label (Round 1's H4: unearned), then Tensional (Round 2's H5: quoted but never argued against the candidate, whose own results ran the other way). Rather than let a third attempt be made by the thread holding the pen, the project lead escalated the classification on 2026-09-13 and then, on 2026-09-14, directed that the source be read before any ruling. That sequence is the reason this entry can be short about the outcome and long about the route.

**What the source read established.** `Review-Artifacts/Doc04_Gesta_Targeted_Read_2026-09-14.md` reports it in full; the findings that carried the ruling are these. Candidate 5's Persistence verdict rested on an absence claim with two limbs, and **both are falsified**. (i) Augustine's position on the scope of collective episcopal authority is attested in a **second, independent, non-treatise locus** — act 50 of the 411 Conference, file line 126796, normalized *Legatur mandatum nostrum, et intellegent quam cuncta contineat* — answered by Emeritus of Caesarea before and Petilianus of Constantine after, and adjudicated by the imperial *cognitor* Marcellinus reasoning from the series of the mandate. (ii) Inter-episcopal authority structure is **operative and contested among clergy far beyond the two anchor figures**: the seven *actores* per side act under a mandate from the wider episcopal body, twice read into the record; its scope, its forfeiture and whether it may be examined at all are litigated across the acts (the term recurs 58 times); and the subscription region alone carries 250 `episcop-` tokens across dozens of named bishops.

**Why Supporting rather than Primary or Tensional.** Primary requires what CF V7.4 states: organizing the ecology broadly, multiple dimensions depending on it, shaping formation pervasively. Candidate 5 still **fails Formation** — the *Gesta* shows the question operative among bishops, not among catechumens or ordinary believers, and Doc_02 yields no evidence of ordinary-believer formation on it. Supporting requires organizing "significant portions of the ecology," which a formally convened assembly of the African episcopate, operating under a delegated mandate whose scope is litigated on the record, plainly is. Tensional is no longer in question: its operative limb was never argued, and the candidate has no competing or reshaping relation anywhere in §6.

**What the ruling does not disturb.** Doc_01 §5's strand-singular determination stands. The 411 material **is** evidence Doc_01 never weighed, so Doc_01 §8 item 10's reopening trigger is now met on its own terms — and §5 addresses that directly rather than by the route it previously took. What the evidence shows is one episcopate disputing the scope of its own collective mandate before a judge: one authority structure in operation, not the two meaningfully distinct patterns Article 21 defines a strand as requiring. **An earlier version of §5 rested the non-reopening on a claim about what a Tensional gravity is "by definition," and misstated the Framework in doing so; that is withdrawn, and the argument now runs on item 10's trigger and Article 21's "once made, governs all subsequent strand attribution."**

**The reconciliation pass, and the one methodological point worth keeping.** Every site carrying the superseded classification was located **by searching the whole document** (`Tensional|escalat|Candidate 5|Conciliar Authority`, twenty-four lines) rather than by copying any enumeration. That is the direct lesson of Round 4: the Round 3 pass ran an exhaustive destination check over a four-item list it had copied from §3, **and the list itself was the defect** — three further sites asserted the same proposition and were never examined. A check can only be as complete as the set it runs over, so the set is now derived rather than inherited. Twenty-four destinations were re-read at HEAD after the edits; all carry the change, and a residue sweep confirmed the only remaining Tensional references are Candidate 8's own (genuinely Tensional, untouched) and the historical record in Open Item 6 and §8.

**Round 4's findings, addressed in part and named in full.** `Review-Artifacts/Doc04_Round4_Review.md` returned SUBSTANTIAL REVISION REQUIRED — 6 HIGH, 5 MEDIUM, 6 LOW, 3 COSMETIC — with a fix-pass tally against Round 3's twenty of **16 fixed, 4 partial, 0 not fixed**. This pass resolves **H1, H2, H3, H4, H5, H6 and M1 — seven of twenty**. **Thirteen remain outstanding and unfixed: M2, M3, M4, M5, L1, L2, L3, L4, L5, L6, C1, C2, C3.** They are enumerated rather than counted, because five consecutive passes on this document overstated what they had closed and the sixth should not.

**One correction carried from the source read.** Registry row 65's floor of fourteen acts is genuinely a floor: act 14, at file line 125505, printing `Angustinus episcopus`, is a fifteenth instance not on that row's list. Row 65's roster locators are also one line low — the `Language: lat` header drift `Doc02_Round30_Review.md` already root-caused and deliberately declined to patch. **Row 65 is left alone on both counts**, per Round 30's own reasoning that hand-patching would re-stale the moment the fleet-wide fix lands; the source-read artifact cites current lines and names the drift.

**Disposition.** **None.** `Doc_04_Gravity_Discovery.md` remains REVISED and undisposed. Thirteen Round 4 findings are outstanding, this reconciliation pass has not itself been reviewed, and CO-022's precondition for disposition is not met. No disposition is self-applied.

**Escalation check.** Representative-identity: does not apply. Portfolio-level/cross-world: does not apply. Governance/methodology: no new rule proposed; the derived-rather-than-inherited destination set is a condition of this document's own passes, following Round 4's own recommendation against escalating it. **Unresolved tensions: closed** — the single live escalation is resolved by the ruling above.


**[CORRECTION, 2026-09-14, Round 7's own H9 — added in place rather than left standing.]** Every *Gesta* finding this entry asserts as fact is **withdrawn**. Both source reads were audited and withdrawn — `Doc04_Round5_Review.md` found the first overstated, `Doc04_Round6_Review.md` found the second unsound — and no *conclusion* of either is relied on in `Doc_04_Gravity_Discovery.md`; one narrow factual survival is relied on and is named at §7 Open Item 6. Read this entry as the record of what was believed and acted on at the time, not as findings.
### 2026-09-14 (later) — The *Gesta* read rewritten after audit, Doc_04's contradictions fixed, Supporting reaffirmed — and Doc_01 §5's strand finding formally reopened under its own item 10

**Direction.** Project lead, directly in session, 2026-09-14, after `Doc04_Round5_Review.md` found the first source read overstated: keep Supporting, fix the contradictions, redo the *Gesta* read.

**The source read is rewritten, not patched.** `Doc04_Gesta_Targeted_Read_2026-09-14.md` now records both versions and what the first got wrong, because the pattern is worth more than the tidiness. Five errors, all one mechanism:

1. **It never searched `concili-`** — about 100 hits in its own declared span — in a read commissioned to test a candidate named *Conciliar Authority Theory*. It searched `mandat`, `plenari`, `auctoritat`, `primat`. The one obvious term went unrun, and with it the best evidence in the file.
2. **It reversed a passage's polarity.** Line 117505 is Emeritus arguing the mandate is beside the cause of faith; the read paraphrased it neutrally and used it for the opposite conclusion.
3. **It counted Migne's editorial apparatus as conference record.** Footnote-marker density per 500 lines shows the acts proper at 116000–118499 and 125500–130999, with **118500–125499 being prosopographical footnotes**. The read's census span was substantially apparatus, and "Constantinus, line 119042" is a bishop subscribing at the **Lateran Council of 649**, named in footnote (72) to gloss a place-name.
4. **Its `mandat` count was ~45% low** — 35 strict against 64 OCR-tolerant in the acts proper — in the artifact that names too-strict searching as this build's failure mode.
5. **It missed two of Augustine's acts** (230, 252) and misdescribed a third: **act 158 is Augustine's own subscription to the mandate** (*mandatum suscepi et subscripsi*, line 121764), not a debate speech — which explains the anomaly Registry row 65 had flagged without diagnosing. **Augustine speaks in seventeen numbered acts, not fourteen.**

**What the corrected read supports — narrower, and better evidenced.** The Catholic delegation grounds its standing in a *universale concilium*: Aurelius cannot exceed "the limit of this mandate, which the fathers and brothers of the universal council of the catholic Church, constituted here at Carthage, gave us" (act 40, line 117492); the delegates are *electi ab universali catholico concilio* (118113); the same mandate is invoked again at 127075. **And the axis is genuinely contested:** Emeritus answers that the cause of faith "can be pursued by right even without a mandate," since "the whole person of the Church subsists in one" (117497, 117505). Two rival accounts of where authority above the individual bishop resides. **What it does not support, and Doc_04 now says so:** that the question reached ordinary clergy. The acts carry 180 numbered speeches across roughly a dozen real speakers. Candidate 5's Formation test still does not pass.

**Supporting reaffirmed, and argued against all three clauses this time.** **[CORRECTION, 2026-09-14, Round 8.]** The "three clauses" reading is **wrong and withdrawn**: CF V7.4's Supporting definition is **two sentences**, and what this entry counts as a third clause is a *concession* attached to a requirement to pass multiple tests — it neither licenses a failing test nor speaks to Formation. Candidate 5 is classified **Supporting, provisionally**, under the Framework's ambiguous-results provision; see the fifth 2026-09-14 entry below. CF V7.4's Supporting definition has three: organizing significant portions of the ecology; functioning within the context established by primary gravities; and passing multiple tests while "may not demonstrat[ing] the same breadth of dependency or persistence." The first reconciliation argued only the first. **The third clause is the one that matters — it expressly anticipates the narrower dependency that made the earlier reach for Tensional look necessary.** **[FURTHER CORRECTION, Round 9.]** The ambiguous-results basis named here is also withdrawn: CF V7.4's antecedent is "where gravity tests yield ambiguous **results**," and this document's six tests yield six determinate verdicts. Candidate 5 is classified **Supporting on the project lead's ruling of 2026-09-14**. `Doc_04_Superseded_Claims.md` §1 holds every superseded basis.

**A substantive consequence, surfaced rather than argued around.** Doc_01 §8 item 10 makes reopening turn on this document surfacing evidence Doc_01 has not weighed — *"in which case the finding is reopened rather than defended past the evidence."* The first reconciliation certified the trigger **not met** in one paragraph and **met** in the next. It is met: the 411 material is evidence Doc_01 never weighed. **So §5 now reopens the strand-singular finding as item 10 directs, and reaffirms it on the evidence rather than defending it past the evidence.** The reaffirmation rests on Article 21's own definition of a strand — a meaningfully distinct pattern *within a single world* — and on the fact that the Donatist position at 411 belongs to a rival communion already outside this world's boundary per Doc_01 §5's own Article 3 argument, not to a second pattern inside it. **Doc_01's finding is unchanged; its status is not. It has now been formally reopened and reaffirmed by the document Doc_01 itself appointed to do that, and the project lead should know the mechanism fired.**

**Three contradictions fixed, all introduced by the first reconciliation.** §7 Open Item 3 still read "Candidate 5 is classified Tensional." §5 answered the item-10 trigger question in both directions. §7 Open Item 2 instructed that the candidate "should not [be treated] as a Primary or **Supporting**… but should [be treated] as a **Supporting** gravity" — a sentence contradicting itself because its lead and its close were edited separately and never read together.
